\begin{array}{l} \left| \frac {\left\{\int_ {x} \boldsymbol {u} (x) \mathbb {g} (d x) \right\} ^ {\top} \boldsymbol {\sigma} ^ {- 1} \boldsymbol {\xi} ^ {(g)} _ {\varepsilon} ^ {(g)}}{\sqrt {n t} \sigma (\pi ; x)} \right| \leq \frac {\left\| \left\{\int_ {x} \boldsymbol {u} (x) \mathbb {g} (d x) \right\} ^ {\top} \boldsymbol {\sigma} ^ {- 1} \| _ {2} \| \boldsymbol {\xi} ^ {(g)} \| _ {2} | \varepsilon^ {(g)} | \right.}{\sqrt {n t} \sigma (\pi ; x)} \\ \leq (c _ {0} + 2 c ^ {\prime}) \frac {\| \left\{\int_ {x} \boldsymbol {u} (x) \mathbb {g} (d x) \right\} ^ {\top} \boldsymbol {\sigma} ^ {- 1} \| _ {2} \| \boldsymbol {\xi} ^ {(g)} \| _ {2}}{\sqrt {n t} \sigma (\pi ; x)} \leq (c _ {0} + 2 c ^ {\prime}) c ^ {*} \sqrt {l} \frac {\| \left\{\int_ {x} \boldsymbol {u} (x) \mathbb {g} (d x) \right\} ^ {\top} \boldsymbol {\sigma} ^ {- 1} \| _ {2}}{\sqrt {n t} \sigma (\pi ; x)} \\ \leq \frac {\sqrt {3 c _ {0}} (c _ {0} + 2 c ^ {\prime}) c ^ {*}}{\sqrt {\bar {c}}} \frac {\sqrt {l}}{\sqrt {n t}}, \\ \end{array}
$$

where the first inequality follows from cauchy-schwarz inequality, the second inequality is due to (e.42), the third inequality is due to lemma 2 and the fact that  $\| \pmb{\xi}^{(g)}\|_2 \leq \sup_x \| \phi_l(x)\|_2$ , and the last inequality follows from (e.49). since  $l \ll \sqrt{nt} / \log(nt)$ , (a) is proven. to verify (b), notice that

$$
\begin{array}{l} \left| \frac {1}{n t} \sum_ {g = 1} ^ {n t} \frac {| \{\int_ {x} \boldsymbol {u} (x) \mathbb {g} (d x) \} ^ {\top} \boldsymbol {\sigma} ^ {- 1} \boldsymbol {\xi} ^ {(g)} _ {\boldsymbol {\mathcal {e}} ^ {(g)}} | ^ {2}}{\sigma^ {2} (\pi ; x)} - 1 \right| = \frac {1}{\sigma^ {2} (\pi ; x)} \\ \times \left| \left\{\int_ {x} \boldsymbol {u} (x) \mathbb {g} (d x) \right\} ^ {\top} \boldsymbol {\sigma} ^ {- 1} \left\{\frac {1}{n t} \sum_ {g = 1} ^ {n t} (\varepsilon^ {(g)}) ^ {2} \boldsymbol {\xi} ^ {(g)} (\boldsymbol {\xi} ^ {(g)}) ^ {\top} - \boldsymbol {\omega} \right\} (\boldsymbol {\sigma} ^ {\top}) ^ {- 1} \left\{\int_ {x} \boldsymbol {u} (x) \mathbb {g} (d x) \right\} \right| \\ \leq \frac {\| \{\int_ {x} \boldsymbol {u} (x) \mathbb {g} (d x) \} ^ {\top} \boldsymbol {\sigma} ^ {- 1} \| _ {2} ^ {2}}{\sigma^ {2} (\pi ; x)} \left\| \frac {1}{n t} \sum_ {g = 1} ^ {n t} (\varepsilon^ {(g)}) ^ {2} \boldsymbol {\xi} ^ {(g)} (\boldsymbol {\xi} ^ {(g)}) ^ {\top} - \boldsymbol {\omega} \right\| _ {2}. \\ \end{array}
$$

in view of (e.49), it suffices to show

$$
\left\| \frac {1}{n t} \sum_ {g = 1} ^ {n t} (\varepsilon^ {(g)}) ^ {2} \pmb {\xi} ^ {(g)} (\pmb {\xi} ^ {(g)}) ^ {\top} - \pmb {\omega} \right\| _ {2} = o _ {p} (1). \tag {e.53}
$$

this can be proven using similar arguments in bounding  $\| \widehat{\pmb{\sigma}} - \pmb{\sigma} \|_2$  in the proof of lemma 3. in view of (e.51) and (e.52), we have by slutsky's theorem that

$$
\frac {\sqrt {n t} \{\widehat {v} (\pi ; \mathbb {g}) - v (\pi ; \mathbb {g}) \}}{\sigma (\pi ; \mathbb {g})} \xrightarrow {d} n (0, 1).
$$

to complete the proof, it remains to show  $\widehat{\sigma} (\pi ;\mathbb{g}) / \sigma (\pi ;\mathbb{g})\stackrel {p}{\to}1$ . using similar arguments in verifying (b), it suffices to show  $\| \widehat{\sigma}^{-1}\widehat{\omega} (\widehat{\sigma}^{\top})^{-1} - \sigma^{-1}\omega (\sigma^{\top})^{-1}\|_{2} = o_{p}(1)$ . by (e.42) and lemma 4, we have

$$
\lambda_ {\max} (\omega) \leq (c _ {0} + 2 c ^ {\prime}) ^ {2} \lambda_ {\max} \left(\frac {1}{t} \sum_ {t = 0} ^ {t - 1} \mathbb {e} \pmb {\xi} _ {0, t} \pmb {\xi} _ {0, t} ^ {\top}\right) = o (1),
$$

and hence  $\| \omega \|_2 = o(1)$ . this together with lemma 3 and the condition  $l \ll \sqrt{nt} / \log(nt)$  yields that

$$
\begin{array}{l} \| \widehat {\pmb {\sigma}} ^ {- 1} \pmb {\omega} (\widehat {\pmb {\sigma}} ^ {\top}) ^ {- 1} - \pmb {\sigma} ^ {- 1} \pmb {\omega} (\pmb {\sigma} ^ {\top}) ^ {- 1} \| _ {2} \leq \| \widehat {\pmb {\sigma}} ^ {- 1} - \pmb {\sigma} \| _ {2} \| \pmb {\omega} \| _ {2} \| (\widehat {\pmb {\sigma}} ^ {\top}) ^ {- 1} \| _ {2} + \| \pmb {\sigma} ^ {- 1} \| _ {2} \| \pmb {\omega} \| _ {2} \| \widehat {\pmb {\sigma}} ^ {- 1} - \pmb {\sigma} \| _ {2} \\ = o _ {p} \{l ^ {1 / 2} (n t) ^ {- 1 / 2} \log (n t) \} = o _ {p} (1). \\ \end{array}
$$

thus, it remains to show  $\| \widehat{\pmb{\sigma}}^{-1}\widehat{\pmb{\omega}} (\widehat{\pmb{\sigma}}^{\top})^{-1} - \widehat{\pmb{\sigma}}^{-1}\pmb {\omega}(\widehat{\pmb{\sigma}}^{\top})^{-1}\|_{2} = o_{p}(1)$ , or  $\| \widehat{\pmb{\omega}} -\pmb {\omega}\| _2 = o_p(1)$  by lemma 3. in view of (e.53), it suffices to show  $\| (nt)^{-1}\sum_{g = 1}^{nt}(\varepsilon^{(g)})^2\pmb{\xi}^{(g)}(\pmb{\xi}^{(g)})^\top -\widehat{\pmb{\omega}}\| _2 =$

$o_p(1)$ , or equivalently,

$$
\sup _ {a \in \mathbb {s} ^ {m l - 1}} \left| \frac {1}{n t} \sum_ {g = 1} ^ {n t} \pmb {a} ^ {\top} \pmb {\xi} ^ {(g)} (\pmb {\xi} ^ {(g)}) ^ {\top} \pmb {a} \{(\varepsilon^ {(g)}) ^ {2} - (\widehat {\varepsilon} ^ {(g)}) ^ {2} \} \right| = o _ {p} (1),
$$

where

$$
\widehat {\varepsilon} ^ {(g)} = y _ {i (g), t (g)} + \gamma \sum_ {a \in \mathcal {a}} \phi_ {l} ^ {\top} (x _ {i (g), t (g) + 1}) \widehat {\beta} _ {a} \pi (a | x _ {i (g), t (g) + 1}) - \phi_ {l} ^ {\top} (x _ {i (g), t (g)}) \widehat {\beta} _ {a _ {i (g), t (g)}}.
$$

by lemma 4, we have  $\sup_{a\in \mathbb{s}^{ml - 1}}(nt)^{-1}\sum_{g = 1}^{nt}\pmb{a}^{\top}\pmb{\xi}^{(g)}(\pmb{\xi}^{(g)})^{\top}\pmb{a} = o_p(1)$ . hence, it suffices to show  $\max_{1\leq g\leq nt}|\left(\varepsilon^{(g)}\right)^2 -\left(\widehat{\varepsilon}^{(g)}\right)^2 | = o_p(1)$ . suppose we have shown that  $\max_{1\leq g\leq nt}|\varepsilon^{(g)} - \widehat{\varepsilon}^{(g)}| = o_p(1)$ . by (e.42),  $\varepsilon^{(g)}$ s are uniformly bounded with probability 1 and thus we have  $\max_{1\leq g\leq nt}|\varepsilon^{(g)} + \widehat{\varepsilon}^{(g)}| = o_p(1)$ . it follows that

$$
\max _ {1 \leq g \leq n t} | (\varepsilon^ {(g)}) ^ {2} - (\widehat {\varepsilon} ^ {(g)}) ^ {2} | \leq \max _ {1 \leq g \leq n t} | \varepsilon^ {(g)} - \widehat {\varepsilon} ^ {(g)} | \max _ {1 \leq g \leq n t} | \varepsilon^ {(g)} + \widehat {\varepsilon} ^ {(g)} | = o _ {p} (1).
$$

therefore, it remains to show  $\max_{1\leq g\leq nt}|\varepsilon^{(g)} - \widehat{\varepsilon}^{(g)}| = o_p(1)$ , or equivalently,

$$
\begin{array}{l} \max  _ {1 \leq i \leq n, 0 \leq t \leq t - 1} \left| \gamma \sum_ {a \in \mathcal {a}} q \left(\pi ; x _ {i, t + 1}, a\right) \pi (a | x _ {i, t + 1}) - q \left(\pi ; x _ {i, t}, a _ {i, t}\right) \right. \tag {e.54} \\ \left. - \gamma \sum_ {a \in \mathcal {a}} \phi_ {l} ^ {\top} \left(x _ {i, t + 1}\right) \widehat {\beta} _ {a} \pi (a | x _ {i, t + 1}) - \phi_ {l} ^ {\top} \left(x _ {i, t}\right) \widehat {\beta} _ {a _ {i, t}} \right| = o _ {p} (1). \\ \end{array}
$$

the lhs of (e.54) is upper bound by

$$
(1 + \gamma) \sup _ {x \in \mathbb {x}, a \in \mathcal {a}} | q (\pi ; x, a) - \phi_ {l} ^ {\top} (x) \widehat {\beta} _ {a} |.
$$

by (e.41), (e.46) and lemma 2, we have

$$
\begin{array}{l} \sup_{x\in \mathbb{x},a\in \mathcal{a}}|q(\pi ;x,a) - \phi_{l}^{\top}(x)\widehat{\beta}_{a}|\leq \sup_{x\in \mathbb{x},a\in \mathcal{a}}|q(\pi ;x,a) - \phi_{l}^{\top}(x)\beta_{a}^{*}| + \sup_{x\in \mathbb{x}}|\phi_{l}^{\top}(x)\widehat{\beta}_{a} - \phi_{l}^{\top}(x)\beta_{a}^{*}| \\ \leq c l ^ {- p / d} + \sup  _ {x \in \mathbb {x}} \| \phi_ {l} (x) \| _ {2} \sup  _ {a \in \mathcal {a}} \| \widehat {\beta} _ {a} - \beta_ {a} ^ {*} \| _ {2} = o (l ^ {- p / d}) + o (l ^ {1 / 2}) \sup  _ {a \in \mathcal {a}} \| \widehat {\beta} _ {a} - \beta_ {a} ^ {*} \| _ {2} \\ = o _ {p} (l ^ {1 / 2 - p / d}) + o _ {p} (l n ^ {- 1 / 2} t ^ {- 1 / 2}). \\ \end{array}
$$

under the given conditions, we have  $l^{p / d} \gg \sqrt{nt}$  and  $l \ll \sqrt{nt} / \log (nt)$ . this implies  $o_{p}(l^{1 / 2 - p / d}) = o_{p}(1)$ , and  $o_{p}(ln^{-1 / 2}t^{-1 / 2}) = o_{p}(1)$ . therefore, we have

$$
\sup  _ {x \in \mathbb {x}, a \in \mathcal {a}} | q (\pi ; x, a) - \phi_ {l} ^ {\top} (x) \widehat {\beta} _ {a} | = o _ {p} (1).
$$

the proof is hence completed.

# e.6 proof of lemma 2

for b-spline basis, the assertion in (e.40) follows from the arguments used in the proof of theorem 3.3 of burman and chen (1989). for wavelet basis, the assertion in (e.40) follows from the arguments used in the proof of theorem 5.1 of chen and christensen (2015).

for either b-spline or wavelet sieve and any  $l \geq 1$ ,  $x \in \mathbb{x}$ , the number of nonzero elements in the vector  $\phi_l(x)$  is bounded by some constant. moreover, each of the basis function is uniformly bounded by  $o(\sqrt{l})$ . this proves that the second assertion.

# e.7 proof of lemma 3

we consider two scenarios: (i)  $t$  grows to infinity; (ii)  $t$  is bounded. the proof is divided into four parts. in the first part, we show  $\pmb{a}^{\top}\pmb{\sigma}\pmb{a} \geq \bar{c}\|\pmb{a}\|_2^2/2$  for any  $\pmb{a} \in \mathbb{r}^{ml}$  and  $\|\pmb{\sigma}^{-1}\|_2 \leq 2/\bar{c}$ , as either  $n \to \infty$ , or  $t \to \infty$ . in the second part, we bound  $\|\widehat{\pmb{\sigma}} - \pmb{\sigma}\|_2$ . in the third part, we bound  $\|\widehat{\pmb{\sigma}}^{-1} - \pmb{\sigma}^{-1}\|_2$ . finally, we show  $\|\pmb{\sigma}\|_2 = o(1)$ .

part 1: it follows from cauchy-schwarz inequality that

$$
\int_ {x \in \mathbb {x}} \sum_ {a \in \mathcal {a}} \{\pmb {a} ^ {\top} \pmb {\xi} (x, a) \} [ \mathbb {e} \{\pmb {u} (x _ {0, 1}) | x _ {0, 0} = x, a _ {0, 0} = a \} ^ {\top} \pmb {a} ] b (a | x) \bar {\mu} (x) d x \leq \eta_ {1} ^ {1 / 2} (\pmb {a}) \eta_ {2} ^ {1 / 2} (\pmb {a}),
$$

where  $\bar{\mu} = t^{-1}\sum_{t=0}^{t-1}\mu_t$ ,  $\mu_t$  is the marginal density of  $x_{0,t}$  and

$$
\eta_ {1} (\pmb {a}) = \int_ {x \in \mathbb {x}} \sum_ {a \in \mathcal {a}} \{\pmb {a} ^ {\top} \pmb {u} (x, a) \} ^ {2} b (a | x) \bar {\mu} (x) d x, \eta_ {2} (\pmb {a}) = \int_ {x \in \mathbb {x}} \sum_ {a \in \mathcal {a}} \{\pmb {a} ^ {\top} \pmb {\xi} (x, a) \} ^ {2} b (a | x) \bar {\mu} (x) d x.
$$

therefore,

$$
\pmb {a} ^ {\top} \pmb {\sigma} \pmb {a} \geq \eta_ {2} (\pmb {a}) - \sqrt {\eta_ {2} (\pmb {a})} \sqrt {\gamma^ {2} \eta_ {1} (\pmb {a})} = \sqrt {\eta_ {2} (\pmb {a})} \frac {\eta_ {2} (\pmb {a}) - \gamma^ {2} \eta_ {1} (\pmb {a})}{\sqrt {\eta_ {2} (\pmb {a})} + \gamma \sqrt {\eta_ {1} (\pmb {a})}}.
$$

under a3(i), we obtain  $\eta_2(\pmb{a}) - \gamma^2\eta_1(\pmb{a}) \geq \bar{c}\|\pmb{a}\|_2^2$  for  $\pmb{a} \in \mathbb{r}^{ml}$ . it follows that  $\gamma \sqrt{\eta_1(\pmb{a})} \leq \sqrt{\eta_2(\pmb{a})}$  and

$$
\boldsymbol {a} ^ {\top} \boldsymbol {\sigma} \boldsymbol {a} \geq \frac {\bar {c} \| \boldsymbol {a} \| _ {2} ^ {2} \sqrt {\eta_ {2} (\boldsymbol {a})}}{\sqrt {\eta_ {2} (\boldsymbol {a})} + \gamma \sqrt {\eta_ {1} (\boldsymbol {a})}} \geq \frac {\bar {c}}{2} \| \boldsymbol {a} \| _ {2} ^ {2}, \quad \forall \boldsymbol {a} \in \mathbb {r} ^ {m l}. \tag {e.55}
$$

we now show

$$
\left\| \boldsymbol {\sigma} \boldsymbol {a} \right\| _ {2} \geq \frac {\bar {c}}{2} \| \boldsymbol {a} \| _ {2}, \quad \forall \boldsymbol {a} \in \mathbb {r} ^ {m l}. \tag {e.56}
$$

otherwise, there exists some  $\pmb{a}_0 \in \mathbb{r}^{ml}$  such that  $\| \sigma \pmb{a}_0 \|_2 < 2^{-1} \bar{c} \| \pmb{a}_0 \|_2$ . by cauchy-schwarz inequality, we obtain  $\pmb{a}_0^\top \pmb{\sigma} \pmb{a}_0 \leq \| \pmb{a}_0 \|_2 \| \pmb{\sigma} \pmb{a}_0 \|_2 < 2^{-1} \bar{c} \| \pmb{a}_0 \|_2^2$ . however, this violates the assertion in (e.55). (e.56) is thus proven.

according to the singular value decomposition, we have  $\pmb{\sigma} = \pmb{v}_1^\top \pmb{\lambda}\pmb{v}_2$  for some orthogonal matrices  $v_{1}, v_{2}$  and some diagonal matrix  $\pmb{\lambda}$ . by orthogonality, we obtain  $\| \pmb{\sigma}\pmb{a}\|_2 = \| \pmb{\lambda}\pmb{v}_2\pmb{a}\|_2$  and  $\| \pmb{a}\|_2 = \| \pmb{v}_2\pmb{a}\|_2$ . in view of (e.56), we have  $\| \pmb{\lambda}\pmb{a}\|_2 \geq 2^{-1}\bar{c}\| \pmb{a}\|_2, \forall \pmb{a} \in \mathbb{r}^{ml}$ . this implies that the absolute value of each diagonal element in  $\pmb{\lambda}$  is at least  $\bar{c}$ . thus, we obtain  $\| \pmb{\lambda}^{-1}\|_2 \leq 2\bar{c}^{-1}$  and hence  $\| \pmb{\sigma}^{-1}\|_2 \leq 2\bar{c}^{-1}$ .

part 2: we first consider scenario (ii). define the random matrix

$$
\pmb {r} _ {i} = \frac {1}{t} \sum_ {t = 0} ^ {t - 1} \pmb {\xi} (x _ {i, t}, a _ {i, t}) \{\pmb {\xi} (x _ {i, t}, a _ {i, t}) - \gamma \pmb {u} (x _ {i, t + 1}) \} ^ {\top}.
$$

by lemma 2, we have  $\max_{1\leq i\leq n,0\leq t\leq t - 1}\| \pmb {\xi}(x_{i,t},a_{i,t})\| _2\leq \sup_x\| \phi_l(x)\| _2\leq c^*\sqrt{l}$  and  $\max_{1\leq i\leq n,1\leq t\leq t}\| \pmb {u}(x_{i,t + 1})\| _2\leq \sup_x\| \phi_l(x)\| _2\leq c^*\sqrt{l}$ . it follows that

$$
\max  _ {1 \leq i \leq n} \| \boldsymbol {r} _ {i} - \mathbb {e} \boldsymbol {r} _ {i} \| _ {2} \leq \frac {2}{t} \sum_ {t = 0} ^ {t - 1} c ^ {*} \sqrt {l} \left(c ^ {*} \sqrt {l} + \gamma c ^ {*} \sqrt {l}\right) \leq 4 l \left(c ^ {*}\right) ^ {2}. \tag {e.57}
$$

let

$$
\begin{array}{l} \sigma_ {n} ^ {2} = \max  \left\{\left\| \sum_ {i = 1} ^ {n} \mathbb {e} (\boldsymbol {r} _ {i} - \mathbb {e} \boldsymbol {r} _ {i}) (\boldsymbol {r} _ {i} - \mathbb {e} \boldsymbol {r} _ {i}) ^ {\top} \right\| _ {2}, \left\| \sum_ {i = 1} ^ {n} \mathbb {e} (\boldsymbol {r} _ {i} - \mathbb {e} \boldsymbol {r} _ {i}) ^ {\top} (\boldsymbol {r} _ {i} - \mathbb {e} \boldsymbol {r} _ {i}) \right\| _ {2} \right\} \\ { = } { n \operatorname* { m a x } \left\{ \left\| \mathbb { e } ( \pmb { r } _ { 0 } - \mathbb { e } \pmb { r } _ { 0 } ) ( \pmb { r } _ { 0 } - \mathbb { e } \pmb { r } _ { 0 } ) ^ { \top } \right\| _ { 2 } , \left\| \mathbb { e } ( \pmb { r } _ { 0 } - \mathbb { e } \pmb { r } _ { 0 } ) ^ { \top } ( \pmb { r } _ { 0 } - \mathbb { e } \pmb { r } _ { 0 } ) \right\| _ { 2 } \right\} . } \\ \end{array}
$$

for any  $\pmb{v} \in \mathbb{r}^{ml}$ , we have

$$
\pmb {v} ^ {\top} \mathbb {e} (\pmb {r} _ {0} - \mathbb {e} \pmb {r} _ {0}) (\pmb {r} _ {0} - \mathbb {e} \pmb {r} _ {0}) ^ {\top} \pmb {v} = \pmb {v} ^ {\top} \mathbb {e} \pmb {r} _ {0} \pmb {r} _ {0} ^ {\top} \pmb {v} - \pmb {v} ^ {\top} (\mathbb {e} \pmb {r} _ {0}) (\mathbb {e} \pmb {r} _ {0}) ^ {\top} \pmb {v} \leq \pmb {v} ^ {\top} \mathbb {e} \pmb {r} _ {0} \pmb {r} _ {0} ^ {\top} \pmb {v}.
$$

moreover, using similar arguments in proving (e.57), we can show

$$
\begin{array}{l} \boldsymbol {v} ^ {\top} \mathbb {e} \boldsymbol {r} _ {0} \boldsymbol {r} _ {0} ^ {\top} \boldsymbol {v} \leq \mathbb {e} \left\| \frac {1}{t} \sum_ {t = 0} ^ {t - 1} \boldsymbol {v} ^ {\top} \boldsymbol {\xi} (x _ {0, t}, a _ {0, t}) \{\boldsymbol {\xi} (x _ {0, t}, a _ {0, t}) - \gamma \boldsymbol {u} (x _ {0, t + 1}) \} ^ {\top} \right\| _ {2} ^ {2} \\ \leq 4 l (c ^ {*}) ^ {2} \mathbb {e} \left\{\frac {1}{t} \sum_ {t = 0} ^ {t - 1} \boldsymbol {v} ^ {\top} \boldsymbol {\xi} \left(x _ {0, t}, a _ {0, t}\right) \right\} ^ {2}. \\ \end{array}
$$

by cauchy-schwarz inequality, we obtain

$$
\begin{array}{l} \boldsymbol {v} ^ {\top} \mathbb {e} \boldsymbol {r} _ {0} \boldsymbol {r} _ {0} ^ {\top} \boldsymbol {v} \leq \frac {4 l (c ^ {*}) ^ {2}}{t} \sum_ {t = 0} ^ {t - 1} \mathbb {e} \{\boldsymbol {v} ^ {\top} \boldsymbol {\xi} (x _ {0, t}, a _ {0, t}) \} ^ {2} \\ \leq \frac {4 l (c ^ {*}) ^ {2} \| \pmb {v} ^ {2} \| _ {2}}{t} \sum_ {t = 0} ^ {t - 1} \lambda_ {\max} \left\{\mathbb {e} \pmb {\xi} (x _ {0, t}, a _ {0, t}) \pmb {\xi} (x _ {0, t}, a _ {0, t}) ^ {\top} \right\}. \\ \end{array}
$$

similarly, we can show

$$
\boldsymbol {v} ^ {\top} \mathbb {e} \boldsymbol {r} _ {0} ^ {\top} \boldsymbol {r} _ {0} \boldsymbol {v} \leq \frac {4 l (c ^ {*}) ^ {2} \| \boldsymbol {v} ^ {2} \| _ {2}}{t} \sum_ {t = 0} ^ {t - 1} \lambda_ {\max } \{\mathbb {e} \boldsymbol {\xi} (x _ {0, t}, a _ {0, t}) \boldsymbol {\xi} (x _ {0, t}, a _ {0, t}) ^ {\top} \},
$$

and hence

$$
\sigma_ {n} ^ {2} \leq \frac {4 l n (c ^ {*}) ^ {2}}{t} \sum_ {t = 0} ^ {t - 1} \lambda_ {\max } \left\{\mathbb {e} \boldsymbol {\xi} \left(x _ {0, t}, a _ {0, t}\right) \boldsymbol {\xi} \left(x _ {0, t}, a _ {0, t}\right) ^ {\top} \right\}. \tag {e.58}
$$

consider  $\lambda_{\mathrm{max}}\{\mathbb{e}\pmb {\xi}(x_{0,0},a_{0,0})\pmb {\xi}(x_{0,0},a_{0,0})^{\top}\}$  first. notice that  $\mathbb{e}\pmb {\xi}(x_{0,0},a_{0,0})\pmb {\xi}(x_{0,0},a_{0,0})^{\top}$  is a block diagonal matrix. for any  $\pmb {v}\in \mathbb{r}^{ml}$ , let  $\pmb {v} = (a_1^\top ,\dots ,a_m^\top)^\top$  where all the sub-vectors  $a_{j}$ s have the same length. with some calculations, we have

$$
\begin{array}{l} \pmb {v} ^ {\top} \mathbb {e} \pmb {\xi} (x _ {0, 0}, a _ {0, 0}) \pmb {\xi} (x _ {0, 0}, a _ {0, 0}) ^ {\top} \pmb {v} = \sum_ {j = 1} ^ {m} \mathbb {e} \{a _ {j} ^ {\top} \phi_ {l} (x _ {0, 0}) \} ^ {2} b (j | x _ {0, 0}) \\ \leq \lambda_ {\max} \bigl \{\mathbb {e} \phi_ {l} (x _ {0, 0}) \phi_ {l} ^ {\top} (x _ {0, 0}) \bigr \} \| \pmb {v} \| _ {2} ^ {2} \leq \lambda_ {\max} \left\{\int_ {x \in \mathbb {x}} \phi_ {l} (x) \phi_ {l} ^ {\top} (x) \nu_ {0} (x) d x \right\} \| \pmb {v} \| _ {2} ^ {2}. \\ \end{array}
$$

by condition a2 and lemma 2, we obtain

$$
\lambda_ {\max} \left\{\int_ {x \in \mathbb {x}} \phi_ {l} (x) \phi_ {l} ^ {\top} (x) \nu_ {0} (x) d x \right\} \leq \sup _ {x \in \mathbb {x}} \nu_ {0} (x) \lambda_ {\max} \left\{\int_ {x \in \mathbb {x}} \phi_ {l} (x) \phi_ {l} ^ {\top} (x) d x \right\} \preceq 1.
$$

this yields

$$
\lambda_ {\max } \left\{\mathbb {e} \boldsymbol {\xi} \left(x _ {0, 0}, a _ {0, 0}\right) \boldsymbol {\xi} \left(x _ {0, 0}, a _ {0, 0}\right) ^ {\top} \right\} \preceq 1. \tag {e.59}
$$

for any  $t > 0$ , the marginal density function of  $x_{0,t}$  is given by

$$
\mu_ {t} (x) = \int_ {x _ {0}, \dots , x _ {t - 1} \in \mathbb {x}} \nu_ {0} \left(x _ {0}\right) q _ {x} \left(x _ {1} \mid x _ {0}\right) \dots q _ {x} \left(x _ {t - 1} \mid x _ {t - 2}\right) q _ {x} \left(x \mid x _ {t - 1}\right) d x _ {0} d x _ {1} \dots d _ {x _ {t - 1}}. \tag {e.60}
$$

thus, we have  $\mu_t(x) \leq \sup_{x', x''} q_x(x'|x'')$  for any  $t \geq 1$  and  $x \in \mathbb{x}$ . under condition a1, we can show the density function  $q_x(x|x')$  is uniformly bounded for any  $x$  and  $x'$ . it follows

that  $\mu_t(x)$  is uniformly bounded for any  $t \geq 1$  and  $x$ . using similar arguments in proving (e.59), we can show

$$
\lambda_ {\max } \left\{\mathbb {e} \boldsymbol {\xi} \left(x _ {0, t}, a _ {0, t}\right) \boldsymbol {\xi} \left(x _ {0, t}, a _ {0, t}\right) ^ {\top} \right\} \preceq 1, \quad \forall 1 \leq t \leq t. \tag {e.61}
$$

this together with (e.58) and (e.59) yields

$$
\sigma_ {n} ^ {2} \leq c n l, \tag {e.62}
$$

for some constant  $c > 0$ . combining this together with (e.57), an application of the matrix concentration inequality (see theorem 1.6 in tropp, 2012) yields that

$$
\operatorname * {p r} \left(\left\| \sum_ {i = 1} ^ {n} \boldsymbol {r} _ {i} - n \mathbb {e} \boldsymbol {r} _ {0} \right\| _ {2} \geq \tau\right) \leq 2 m l \exp \left(- \frac {\tau^ {2}}{c n l + 8 l (c ^ {*}) ^ {2} \tau / 3}\right), \quad \forall \tau > 0.
$$

set  $\tau = 3\sqrt{cnl\log n}$ . since  $t$  is bounded, under the given conditions,  $n$  will grow to infinity. for sufficiently large  $n$ , we have  $8l(c^{*})^{2}\tau /3\ll \tau^{2}$  and hence

$$
\pr \left(\left\| \sum_ {i = 1} ^ {n} \boldsymbol {r} _ {i} - n \mathbb {e} \boldsymbol {r} _ {0} \right\| _ {2} \geq 3 \sqrt {c n l \log n}\right) \leq \frac {2 m l}{n ^ {4}}.
$$

since  $l \ll n$  and  $t$  is bounded, we obtain  $2ml / n^4 \ll 1 / (n^2 t^2)$ . thus, we can show that the following event occurs with probability at least  $1 - o(n^{-2}t^{-2})$ ,

$$
\left\| \widehat {\boldsymbol {\sigma}} - \boldsymbol {\sigma} \right\| _ {2} = \frac {1}{n} \left\| \sum_ {i = 1} ^ {n} \boldsymbol {r} _ {i} - n \mathbb {e} \boldsymbol {r} _ {0} \right\| _ {2} \preceq \sqrt {(n t) ^ {- 1} l \log (n t)}, \tag {e.63}
$$

since  $t$  is bounded.

now let's consider scenario (i). let

$$
\pmb {r} _ {i, t} = \pmb {\xi} (x _ {i, t}, a _ {i, t}) \{\pmb {\xi} (x _ {i, t}, a _ {i, t}) - \gamma \pmb {u} (x _ {i, t + 1}) \} ^ {\top}, \quad \forall i, t.
$$

we aim to apply the matrix concentration inequality to the sum of independent random matrix (regardless of whether  $n$  is bounded or not),

$$
\frac {1}{n} \sum_ {i = 1} ^ {n} \left\{\frac {1}{t} \sum_ {t = 0} ^ {t - 1} (\pmb {r} _ {i, t} - \pmb {\sigma}) \right\}.
$$

we begin by providing an upper error bound for  $\max_{1\leq i\leq n}\| t^{-1}\sum_{t = 0}^{t - 1}(\pmb{r}_{i,t} - \pmb {\sigma})\| _2$ . let  $\mathcal{f}_{t - 1} = \{(x_{0,j},a_{0,j})\}_{0\leq j\leq t}$ , for all  $t\geq 0$ , and  $\sigma (\mathcal{f}_t)$  be the  $\sigma$ -algebra generated by  $\mathcal{f}_t$ . define

$$
\pmb {r} _ {0, t} ^ {*} = \pmb {\xi} (x _ {0, t}, a _ {0, t}) \{\pmb {\xi} (x _ {0, t}, a _ {0, t}) - \gamma \pmb {u} (x _ {0, t}, a _ {0, t}) \} ^ {\top}.
$$

the sum  $\sum_{t=0}^{t-1} (\pmb{r}_{0,t}^* - \pmb{r}_{0,t})$  forms a mean zero matrix martingale with respect to the filtration  $\{\sigma(\mathcal{f}_t) : t \geq -1\}$ . similar to (e.57) and (e.62), we can show

$$
\max _ {0 \leq t \leq t - 1} \| \boldsymbol {r} _ {0, t} - \boldsymbol {r} _ {0, t} ^ {*} \| _ {2} \leq 4 l (c ^ {*}) ^ {2},
$$

$$
\max  \left\{\left\| \sum_ {t = 0} ^ {t - 1} \mathbb {e} \left\{\left(\boldsymbol {r} _ {0, t} - \boldsymbol {r} _ {0, t} ^ {*}\right) ^ {\top} \left(\boldsymbol {r} _ {0, t} - \boldsymbol {r} _ {0, t} ^ {*}\right) \mid \mathcal {f} _ {t - 1} \right\} \right\| _ {2}, \right.
$$

$$
\left\| \sum_ {t = 0} ^ {t - 1} \mathbb {e} \left\{\left(\boldsymbol {r} _ {0, t} - \boldsymbol {r} _ {0, t} ^ {*}\right)\left(\boldsymbol {r} _ {0, t} - \boldsymbol {r} _ {0, t} ^ {*}\right) ^ {\top} | \mathcal {f} _ {t - 1} \right\}\right\| _ {2} \left. \right\} \preceq l t.
$$

by the matrix martingale concentration inequality (corollary 1.3, tropp, 2011), we obtain the following occurs with probability at least  $1 - o(n^{-3}t^{-2})$

$$
\left\| \sum_ {t = 0} ^ {t - 1} \left(\boldsymbol {r} _ {0, t} - \boldsymbol {r} _ {0, t} ^ {*}\right) \right\| _ {2} \preceq \sqrt {l t \log (n t)}. \tag {e.64}
$$

define

$$
\boldsymbol {r} _ {0, t} ^ {* *} = \sum_ {a \in \mathcal {a}} \boldsymbol {\xi} \left(x _ {0, t} ^ {*}, a\right) \left\{\boldsymbol {\xi} \left(x _ {0, t} ^ {*}, a\right) - \gamma \boldsymbol {u} \left(x _ {0, t} ^ {*}, a\right) \right\} ^ {\top} b (a | x _ {0, t} ^ {*}). \tag {e.65}
$$

conditional on  $\{x_{0,t}^{*}\}_{t\geq 0},\{r_{0,t}^{*} - r_{0,t}^{**}\}_{t\geq 0}$  are independent mean zero random variables. using similar arguments in proving (e.63), we can show that

$$
\operatorname * {p r} \left(\left\| \sum_ {t = 0} ^ {t - 1} (\pmb {r} _ {0, t} ^ {*} - \pmb {r} _ {0, t} ^ {* *} \right\| _ {2} \geq c \sqrt {l t \log (n t)} \middle | \{x _ {0, t} ^ {*} \} _ {t \geq 0}\right) = o (n ^ {- 3} t ^ {- 2}),
$$

for some constant  $c > 0$ , where the big- $o$  term is independent of  $\{x_{0,t}^{*}\}_{t\geq 0}$ . thus, we obtain

$$
\operatorname * {p r} \left(\left\| \sum_ {t = 0} ^ {t - 1} (\pmb {r} _ {0, t} ^ {*} - \pmb {r} _ {0, t} ^ {* *} \right\| _ {2} \geq c \sqrt {l t \log (n t)}\right) = o (n ^ {- 3} t ^ {- 2}),
$$

this together with (e.64) implies that the following event occurs with probability at least  $1 - o(n^{-3}t^{-2})$ ,

$$
\left\| \sum_ {t = 0} ^ {t - 1} \left(\boldsymbol {r} _ {0, t} - \boldsymbol {r} _ {0, t} ^ {* *}\right) \right\| _ {2} \preceq \sqrt {l t \log (n t)}. \tag {e.66}
$$

notice that each  $\pmb{r}_{0,t}^{**}$  is a function of  $x_{0,t}$  only, with mean  $\pmb{\sigma}$ . following davydov (1973), define the  $\beta$ -mixing coefficient of the stationary markov chain  $\{x_{0,t}\}_{t\geq 0}$  as

$$
\beta (q) = \int_ {x \in \mathbb {x}} \sup  _ {0 \leq \varphi \leq 1} | \mathbb {e} \{\varphi (x _ {0, q}) | x _ {0, 0} = x \} - \mathbb {e} \varphi (x _ {0, 0}) | \mu (x) d x.
$$

under the geometric ergodicity assumption in a3(ii) and , it follows from lemma 1 of meitz and saikkonen (2019) that  $\{x_{0,t}\}_{t\geq 0}$  is exponentially  $\beta$  -mixing. that is,  $\beta (t) = o(\rho^t)$  for some  $\rho < 1$  and any  $t\geq 0$ . using similar arguments in proving (e.57), we can show

$$
\max _ {0 \leq t \leq t - 1} \| \boldsymbol {r} _ {0, t} ^ {* *} - \boldsymbol {\sigma} \| _ {2} \leq 4 l (c ^ {*}) ^ {2}. \tag {e.67}
$$

moreover, for any  $0 \leq t_1 \leq t_2 \leq t - 1$  and any  $\pmb{v}_1, \pmb{v}_2 \in \mathbb{r}^{ml}$ , we have by cauchy-schwarz inequality that

$$
\begin{array}{l} | \pmb {v} _ {1} ^ {\top} \mathbb {e} (\pmb {r} _ {0, t _ {1}} ^ {* *} - \pmb {\sigma}) (\pmb {r} _ {0, t _ {2}} ^ {* *} - \pmb {\sigma}) ^ {\top} \pmb {v} _ {2} | \leq \sqrt {\mathbb {e} \| \pmb {v} _ {1} ^ {\top} (\pmb {r} _ {0 , t _ {1}} ^ {* *} - \pmb {\sigma}) \| _ {2} ^ {2}} \sqrt {\mathbb {e} \| \pmb {v} _ {2} ^ {\top} (\pmb {r} _ {0 , t _ {2}} ^ {* *} - \pmb {\sigma}) \| _ {2} ^ {2}} \\ \leq \sqrt {\lambda_ {\max} \{\mathbb {e} (\pmb {r} _ {0 , t _ {1}} ^ {* *} - \pmb {\sigma}) (\pmb {r} _ {0 , t _ {1}} ^ {* *} - \pmb {\sigma}) ^ {\top} \}} \sqrt {\lambda_ {\max} \{\mathbb {e} (\pmb {r} _ {0 , t _ {2}} ^ {* *} - \pmb {\sigma}) (\pmb {r} _ {0 , t _ {2}} ^ {* *} - \pmb {\sigma}) ^ {\top} \}} \| \pmb {v} _ {1} \| _ {2} \| \pmb {v} _ {2} \| _ {2}. \\ \end{array}
$$

using similar arguments in proving (e.62), we can show

$$
\max _ {0 \leq t \leq t - 1} \lambda_ {\max} \{\mathbb {e} (\pmb {r} _ {0, t} ^ {* *} - \pmb {\sigma}) (\pmb {r} _ {0, t} ^ {* *} - \pmb {\sigma}) ^ {\top} \} \preceq l.
$$

this implies

$$
\max  _ {0 \leq t _ {1}, t _ {2} \leq t - 1} \sup  _ {\boldsymbol {v} _ {1} \neq 0, \boldsymbol {v} _ {2} \neq 0} \frac {| \boldsymbol {v} _ {1} ^ {\top} \mathbb {e} (\boldsymbol {r} _ {0 , t _ {1}} ^ {* *} - \boldsymbol {\sigma}) (\boldsymbol {r} _ {0 , t _ {2}} ^ {* *} - \boldsymbol {\sigma}) ^ {\top} \boldsymbol {v} _ {2} |}{\| \boldsymbol {v} _ {1} \| _ {2} \| \boldsymbol {v} _ {2} \| _ {2}} \preceq l,
$$

and hence,

$$
\max _ {0 \leq t _ {1}, t _ {2} \leq t - 1} \sup _ {\boldsymbol {v} \neq 0} \frac {\| \mathbb {e} (\boldsymbol {r} _ {0 , t _ {1}} ^ {* *} - \boldsymbol {\sigma}) (\boldsymbol {r} _ {0 , t _ {2}} ^ {* *} - \boldsymbol {\sigma}) ^ {\top} \boldsymbol {v} \| _ {2}}{\| \boldsymbol {v} \| _ {2}} \preceq l,
$$

or equivalently,

$$
\max _ {0 \leq t _ {1}, t _ {2} \leq t - 1} \| \mathbb {e} (\pmb {r} _ {0, t _ {1}} ^ {* *} - \pmb {\sigma}) (\pmb {r} _ {0, t _ {2}} ^ {* *} - \pmb {\sigma}) ^ {\top} \| _ {2} \preceq l.
$$

similarly, we can show

$$
\max  _ {0 \leq t _ {1}, t _ {2} \leq t - 1} \| \mathbb {e} \left(\boldsymbol {r} _ {0, t _ {1}} ^ {* *} - \boldsymbol {\sigma}\right) ^ {\top} \left(\boldsymbol {r} _ {0, t _ {2}} ^ {* * *} - \boldsymbol {\sigma}\right) \| _ {2} \preceq l. \tag {e.68}
$$

let  $\pmb{\sigma}_{t} = \mathbb{e}\pmb{r}_{0,t}$ . notice that  $\pmb{\sigma} = t^{-1}\sum_{t=0}^{t-1}\pmb{\sigma}_{t}$  and we have  $\sum_{t=0}^{t-1}(\pmb{r}_{0,t}-\pmb{\sigma}) = \sum_{t=0}^{t-1}(\pmb{r}_{0,t}-\pmb{\sigma}_{t})$ . similar to theorem 4.2 of chen and christensen (2015), we can show there exist some constant  $c > 0$  such that for any  $\tau \geq 0$  and integer  $1 < q < t$ ,

$$
\begin{array}{l} \operatorname * {p r} \left(\left\| \sum_ {t = 0} ^ {t - 1} (\boldsymbol {r} _ {0, t} ^ {* *} - \boldsymbol {\sigma} _ {t}) \right\| _ {2} \geq 6 \tau\right) \leq \frac {t}{q} \beta (q) + \operatorname * {p r} \left(\left\| \sum_ {t \in \mathcal {i} _ {r}} (\boldsymbol {r} _ {0, t} ^ {* *} - \boldsymbol {\sigma} _ {t}) \right\| _ {2} \geq \tau\right) \\ + 4 m l \exp \left(- \frac {\tau^ {2} / 2}{c t q l + 4 q l \tau (c ^ {*}) ^ {2} / 3}\right), \qquad \mathrm {(e . 6 9)} \\ \end{array}
$$

where  $\mathcal{i}_r = \{q\lfloor (t + 1) / q\rfloor ,q\lfloor (t + 1) / q\rfloor +1,\dots ,t - 1\}$ . suppose  $\tau \geq 5ql(c^{*})^{2}$ . notice that  $|\mathcal{i}_r|\leq q$ . it follows from (e.67) that

$$
\pr \left(\left\| \sum_ {t \in \mathcal {i} _ {r}} \left(\boldsymbol {r} _ {0, t} ^ {* *} - \boldsymbol {\sigma} _ {t}\right) \right\| _ {2} \geq \tau\right) = 0. \tag {e.70}
$$

since  $\beta(q) = o(\rho^q)$ , set  $q = -3\log(nt)/\log\rho$ , we obtain  $t\beta(q)/q = o(n^{-3}t^{-2})$ . set  $\tau = \max\{4\sqrt{ctql\log(tn)}, 11ql(c^*)^2\log(nt)\}$ , we obtain that

$$
\frac {\tau^ {2}}{4} \geq 4 c t q l \log (t n) \mathrm {a n d} \frac {\tau^ {2}}{4} \geq 1 6 q l \tau (c ^ {*}) ^ {2} / 3 \log (t n) \mathrm {a n d} \tau \geq 5 q l (c ^ {*}) ^ {2},
$$

as either  $n \to \infty$  or  $t \to \infty$ . it follows from (e.69), (e.70) and the condition  $l \ll nt$  that the following event occurs with probability at least  $1 - o(n^{-3}t^{-2})$ ,

$$
\left\| \sum_ {t = 0} ^ {t - 1} (\pmb {r} _ {0, t} ^ {* *} - \pmb {\sigma}) \right\| _ {2} \preceq \max \{\sqrt {t l} \log (t n), l \log^ {2} (t n) \}.
$$

combining this together with (e.66) yields that the following event occurs with probability at least  $1 - o(n^{-3}t^{-2})$ ,

$$
\left\| \sum_ {t = 0} ^ {t - 1} (\boldsymbol {r} _ {0, t} - \boldsymbol {\sigma}) \right\| _ {2} \preceq \max  \{\sqrt {t l} \log (t n), l \log^ {2} (t n) \}. \tag {e.71}
$$

by bonferroni's inequality, we obtain with probability at least  $1 - o(n^{-2}t^{-2})$  that

$$
\max _ {i \in \{1, \dots , n \}} \left\| \sum_ {t = 0} ^ {t - 1} (\boldsymbol {r} _ {i, t} - \boldsymbol {\sigma}) \right\| _ {2} \leq \bar {c} \max  \{\sqrt {t l} \log (t n), l \log^ {2} (t n) \}, \tag {e.72}
$$

for some constant  $\bar{c} > 0$ . for  $i = 0, 1, \dots, n$ , let  $\mathcal{a}_i$  denote the event

$$
\mathcal {a} _ {i} = \left\{\left\| \sum_ {t = 0} ^ {t - 1} (\pmb {r} _ {i, t} - \pmb {\sigma}) \right\| _ {2} \leq \bar {c} \max \{\sqrt {t l} \log (t n), l \log^ {2} (t n) \} \right\}.
$$

it follows from (e.72) that the following event occurs with probability at least  $1 - o(n^{-2}t^{-2})$ ,

$$
\sum_ {i = 1} ^ {n} \sum_ {t = 0} ^ {t - 1} \left(\boldsymbol {r} _ {i, t} - \boldsymbol {\sigma}\right) = \sum_ {i = 1} ^ {n} \left\{\sum_ {t = 0} ^ {t - 1} \left(\boldsymbol {r} _ {i, t} - \boldsymbol {\sigma}\right) \right\} \mathbb {i} (\mathcal {a} _ {i}). \tag {e.73}
$$

now we provide an upper error bound for

$$
\left\| \mathbb {e} \left\{\sum_ {t = 0} ^ {t - 1} (\pmb {r} _ {0, t} - \pmb {\sigma}) \right\} ^ {\top} \left\{\sum_ {t = 0} ^ {t - 1} (\pmb {r} _ {0, t} - \pmb {\sigma}) \right\} \right\| _ {2} = \sup _ {\pmb {v} \in \mathbb {s} ^ {m l - 1}} \mathbb {e} \left\| \left\{\sum_ {t = 0} ^ {t - 1} (\pmb {r} _ {0, t} - \pmb {\sigma}) \right\} \pmb {v} \right\| _ {2} ^ {2}.
$$

note that

$$
\begin{array}{l} \sup _ {\boldsymbol {v} \in \mathbb {s} ^ {m l - 1}} \mathbb {e} \boldsymbol {v} ^ {\top} \left\{\sum_ {t = 0} ^ {t - 1} (\boldsymbol {r} _ {0, t} - \boldsymbol {\sigma}) \right\} ^ {\top} \left\{\sum_ {t = 0} ^ {t - 1} (\boldsymbol {r} _ {0, t} - \boldsymbol {\sigma}) \right\} \boldsymbol {v} \\ \leq \underbrace {\sum_ {t = 0} ^ {t - 1} \sup  _ {\boldsymbol {v} \in \mathbb {s} ^ {m l - 1}} \mathbb {e} \boldsymbol {v} ^ {\top} \left(\boldsymbol {r} _ {0 , t} - \boldsymbol {\sigma} _ {t}\right) ^ {\top} \left(\boldsymbol {r} _ {0 , t} - \boldsymbol {\sigma} _ {t}\right) \boldsymbol {v}} _ {\eta_ {1}} \tag {e.74} \\ + \underbrace{\sum_{\substack{0\leq t_{1},t_{2}\leq t - 1\\ 1\leq|t_{1} - t_{2}|\leq 3}}\sup_{\boldsymbol {v}\in\mathbb{s}^{ml- 1}}\mathbb{e}\boldsymbol{v}^{\top}(\boldsymbol{r}_{0,t_{1}} - \boldsymbol{\sigma}_{t_{1}})^{\top}(\boldsymbol{r}_{0,t_{2}} - \boldsymbol{\sigma}_{t_{2}})\boldsymbol{v}}_{\eta_{2}} \\ + \underbrace{\sum_{\substack{0\leq t_{1},t_{2}\leq t - 1\\ |t_{1} - t_{2}|\geq 4}}\sup_{\boldsymbol {v}\in \mathbb{s}^{ml - 1}}\mathbb{e}\boldsymbol{v}^{\top}(\boldsymbol{r}_{0,t_{1}} - \boldsymbol{\sigma}_{t_{1}})^{\top}(\boldsymbol{r}_{0,t_{2}} - \boldsymbol{\sigma}_{t_{2}})\boldsymbol{v}}_{\eta_{3}}. \\ \end{array}
$$

by (e.68), we obtain that

$$
\eta_ {1} \preceq l t \text {a n d} \eta_ {2} \preceq l t. \tag {e.75}
$$

for any  $0 \leq t_1 < t_2 \leq t - 1$  with  $t_2 - t_1 \geq 4$ , it follows from ma that  $(x_{0,t_2}, a_{0,t_2}, x_{0,t_2 + 1})$  is independent of  $(x_{0,t_1}, a_{0,t_1}, x_{0,t_1 + 1})$  given  $x_{0,t_2 - 1}$ . thus, we have

$$
\mathbb {e} \big (\pmb {r} _ {0, t _ {1}} - \pmb {\sigma} _ {t _ {1}} \big) ^ {\top} \big (\pmb {r} _ {0, t _ {2}} - \pmb {\sigma} _ {t _ {2}} \big) = \mathbb {e} \big (\pmb {r} _ {0, t _ {1}} - \pmb {\sigma} _ {t _ {1}} \big) ^ {\top} \mathbb {e} \big \{\big (\pmb {r} _ {0, t _ {2}} - \pmb {\sigma} _ {t _ {2}} \big) | x _ {0, t _ {2} - 1} \big \}.
$$

similarly, conditional on  $x_{0,t_1 + 2}$ ,  $(x_{0,t_1}, a_{0,t_1}, x_{0,t_1 + 1})$  and  $x_{0,t_2 - 1}$  are independent. it follows that

$$
\begin{array}{l} \mathbb {e} \left(\boldsymbol {r} _ {0, t _ {1}} - \boldsymbol {\sigma} _ {t _ {1}}\right) ^ {\top} \mathbb {e} \left\{\left(\boldsymbol {r} _ {0, t _ {2}} - \boldsymbol {\sigma} _ {t _ {2}}\right) \mid x _ {0, t _ {2} - 1} \right\} \\ { = } { \mathbb { e } [ \mathbb { e } \{ ( \boldsymbol { r } _ { 0 , t _ { 1 } } - \boldsymbol { \sigma } _ { t _ { 1 } } ) | x _ { 0 , t _ { 1 } + 2 } \} ] ^ { \top } [ \mathbb { e } \{ ( \boldsymbol { r } _ { 0 , t _ { 2 } } - \boldsymbol { \sigma } _ { t _ { 2 } } ) | x _ { 0 , t _ { 2 } - 1 } \} ] , } \\ \end{array}
$$

and hence

$$
\mathbb {e} \left(\boldsymbol {r} _ {0, t _ {1}} - \boldsymbol {\sigma} _ {t _ {1}}\right) ^ {\top} \left(\boldsymbol {r} _ {0, t _ {2}} - \boldsymbol {\sigma} _ {t _ {2}}\right) = \mathbb {e} \left[ \mathbb {e} \left\{\left(\boldsymbol {r} _ {0, t _ {1}} - \boldsymbol {\sigma} _ {t _ {1}}\right) \mid x _ {0, t _ {1} + 2} \right\} \right] ^ {\top} \left[ \mathbb {e} \left\{\left(\boldsymbol {r} _ {0, t _ {2}} - \boldsymbol {\sigma} _ {t _ {2}}\right) \mid x _ {0, t _ {2} - 1} \right\} \right] \tag {e.76}
$$

define  $\theta_{1,t_1}(x) = \mathbb{e}(\pmb{r}_{0,t_1}|x_{0,t_1 + 2} = x)$  and  $\theta_{2,t_2}(x) = \mathbb{e}(\pmb{r}_{0,t_2}|x_{0,t_2 - 1} = x)$ . let  $\mathbb{e}^{x_{0,t}}$

denote the conditional expectation given  $x_{0,t}$ . with some calculations, we can show

$$
\begin{array}{l} \theta_ {1, t _ {1}} (x) = \mathbb {e} \left[ \mathbb {e} ^ {x _ {0, t _ {1} + 1}} \boldsymbol {\xi} \left(x _ {0, t _ {1}}, a _ {0, t _ {1}}\right) \left\{\boldsymbol {\xi} \left(x _ {0, t _ {1}}, a _ {0, t _ {1}}\right) - \gamma \boldsymbol {u} \left(x _ {0, t _ {1} + 1}\right) \right\} ^ {\top} \mid x _ {0, t _ {1} + 2} = x \right] \\ = \mathbb {e} \left[ \int_ {y \in \mathbb {x}} \sum_ {a \in \mathcal {a}} \boldsymbol {\xi} (y, a) \left\{\boldsymbol {\xi} (y, a) - \gamma \boldsymbol {u} \left(x _ {0, t _ {1} + 1}\right) \right\} ^ {\top} \frac {b (a | y) q \left(x _ {0 , t _ {1} + 1} | y , a\right) \mu_ {t _ {1}} (y)}{\mu_ {t _ {1} + 1} \left(x _ {0 , t _ {1} + 1}\right)} d y \bigg | x _ {0, t _ {1} + 2} = x \right] \\ = \int_ {y _ {1}, y _ {2} \in \mathbb {x}} \sum_ {a \in \mathcal {a}} \boldsymbol {\xi} (y _ {1}, a) \left\{\boldsymbol {\xi} (y _ {1}, a) - \gamma \boldsymbol {u} (y _ {2}) \right\} ^ {\top} b (a | y _ {1}) q (y _ {2} | y _ {1}, a) \frac {\mu_ {t _ {1}} (y _ {1}) q _ {x} (x | y _ {2})}{\mu_ {t _ {1} + 2} (x)} d y _ {1} d y _ {2}, \\ \end{array}
$$

and

$$
\begin{array}{l} \theta_ {2, t _ {2}} (x) = \mathbb {e} \left[ \sum_ {a \in \mathcal {a}} \boldsymbol {\xi} \left(x _ {0, t _ {2}}, a\right) \left\{\boldsymbol {\xi} \left(x _ {0, t _ {2}}, a\right) - \gamma \boldsymbol {u} \left(x _ {0, t _ {2}}, a\right) \right\} ^ {\top} b (a | x _ {0, t _ {2}}) \bigg | x _ {0, t _ {2} - 1} = x \right] \\ = \int_ {y \in \mathbb {x}} \sum_ {a \in \mathcal {a}} \boldsymbol {\xi} (y, a) \left\{\boldsymbol {\xi} (y, a) - \gamma \boldsymbol {u} (y, a) \right\} ^ {\top} b (a | y) q _ {x} (y | x) d y. \\ \end{array}
$$

it follows from (e.76) that

$$
\begin{array}{l} \boldsymbol {v} ^ {\top} \mathbb {e} (\boldsymbol {r} _ {0, t _ {1}} - \boldsymbol {\sigma}) ^ {\top} (\boldsymbol {r} _ {0, t _ {2}} - \boldsymbol {\sigma}) \boldsymbol {v} = \boldsymbol {v} ^ {\top} \mathbb {e} \theta_ {1} ^ {\top} (x _ {0, t _ {1} + 2}) \theta_ {2} (x _ {0, t _ {2} - 1}) \boldsymbol {v} - \boldsymbol {v} ^ {\top} (\boldsymbol {\sigma}) ^ {\top} \boldsymbol {\sigma} \boldsymbol {v} \\ = \boldsymbol {v} ^ {\top} \mathbb {e} \theta_ {1, t _ {1}} ^ {\top} (x _ {0, t _ {1} + 2}) \theta_ {2, t _ {2}} (x _ {0, t _ {2} - 1}) \boldsymbol {v} - \boldsymbol {v} ^ {\top} \left\{\mathbb {e} \theta_ {1, t _ {1}} ^ {\top} (x _ {0, t _ {1} + 2}) \right\} ^ {\top} \mathbb {e} \theta_ {2, t _ {2}} (x _ {0, t _ {2} - 1}) \boldsymbol {v} \\ = \sum_ {j = 1} ^ {m l} \operatorname {c o v} \left\{\boldsymbol {v} ^ {\top} \theta_ {1, t _ {1},.., j} \left(x _ {0, t _ {1} + 2}\right), \theta_ {2, t _ {2}, j, \cdot} \left(x _ {0, t _ {2} - 1}\right) \boldsymbol {v} \right\} \\ \end{array}
$$

where  $\theta_{l,t,j,\cdot}(x)$  and  $\theta_{l,t,\cdot,j}(x)$  denote the  $j$ -th row and  $j$ -column of  $\theta_{l,t}(x)$ , respectively. let  $\xi_j(\cdot, \cdot)$  be the  $j$ -th element of  $\pmb{\xi}(\cdot, \cdot)$ . by lemma 2 and the definitions of  $\pmb{\xi}$  and  $\pmb{u}$ ,

$$
\sup_{\substack{\boldsymbol {v}\in \mathbb{s}^{ml^{-1}},x\in \mathbb{x}\\ a\in \mathcal{a}}}| \boldsymbol{\xi}^{\top}(x,a)\boldsymbol {v}|\preceq l^{1 / 2}\text{and}\sup_{\boldsymbol {v}\in \mathbb{s}^{ml^{-1}},x\in \mathbb{x}}|u^{\top}(x)\boldsymbol {v}|\preceq l^{1 / 2}.
$$

it follows that

$$
\begin{array}{l} \max  _ {t} \max  _ {j \in \{1, \dots , m l \}} \sup  _ {\boldsymbol {v} \in \mathbb {s} ^ {m l - 1}, x \in \mathbb {x}} | \boldsymbol {v} ^ {\top} \boldsymbol {\theta} _ {1, t, \cdot , j} (x) | \\ \succ \int_ {y _ {1}, y _ {2} \in \mathbb {x}} \sum_ {a \in \mathcal {a}} | \xi_ {j} (y _ {1}, a) | | \boldsymbol {v} ^ {\top} \left\{\boldsymbol {\xi} \left(y _ {1}, a\right) - \gamma \boldsymbol {u} \left(y _ {2}\right) \right\} | b (a | y _ {1}) d y _ {1} d y _ {2} \\ \preceq l ^ {1 / 2} \int_ {y _ {1} \in \mathbb {x}} \sum_ {a \in \mathcal {a}} | \xi_ {j} (y _ {1}, a) | b (a | y _ {1}) d y _ {1} \leq l ^ {1 / 2} \max  _ {j \in \{1, \dots , l \}} \int_ {y \in \mathbb {x}} | \phi_ {l, j} (y) | d y. \\ \end{array}
$$

similarly to lemma 2, we can show  $\max_{j\in \{1,\ldots ,l\}}\int_{y\in \mathbb{x}}|\phi_{l,j}(y)|dy\succeq l^{-1 / 2}$ , and hence

$$
\max  _ {j \in \{1, \dots , m l \}} \sup  _ {\boldsymbol {v} \in \mathbb {s} ^ {m l - 1}, x \in \mathbb {x}} | \boldsymbol {v} ^ {\top} \boldsymbol {\theta} _ {1, t, \cdot , j} (x) | \preceq 1. \tag {e.77}
$$

it follows that

$$
\begin{array}{l} \sup _ {\pmb {v} \in \mathbb {s} ^ {m l - 1}} \left| \pmb {v} ^ {\top} \mathbb {e} (\pmb {r} _ {0, t _ {1}} - \pmb {\sigma} _ {t _ {1}}) ^ {\top} (\pmb {r} _ {0, t _ {2}} - \pmb {\sigma} _ {t _ {2}}) \pmb {v} \right| \\ \leq \sup  _ {\boldsymbol {v} \in \mathbb {s} ^ {m l - 1}} \sum_ {j = 1} ^ {m l} \int_ {x \in \mathbb {x}} | \mathbb {e} \left\{\theta_ {2, t, j, \cdot} \left(x _ {0, t _ {2} - 1}\right) \boldsymbol {v} \mid x _ {0, t _ {1} + 2} \right\} - \mathbb {e} \left\{\theta_ {2, t, j, \cdot} \left(x _ {0, t _ {2} - 1}\right) \boldsymbol {v} \right\lvert   | \boldsymbol {v} ^ {\top} \theta_ {1, \cdot , j} (x) | \mu (x) d x \\ \preceq \sup  _ {\boldsymbol {v} \in \mathbb {s} ^ {m l - 1}} \sum_ {j = 1} ^ {m l} \int_ {x \in \mathbb {x}} | \mathbb {e} \{\theta_ {2, t, j, *} (x _ {0, t _ {2} - 1}) \boldsymbol {v} | x _ {0, t _ {1} + 2} \} - \mathbb {e} \{\theta_ {2, t, j, *} (x _ {0, t _ {2} - 1}) \boldsymbol {v} | \mu (x) d x. \\ \end{array}
$$

similar to (e.77), we can show  $\max_t\max_{j\in \{1,\ldots ,ml\}}\sup_{\pmb {v}\in \mathbb{s}^{ml - 1},x\in \mathbb{x}}|\theta_{2,j,\cdot}(x)\pmb {v}|\preceq 1$ . it follows from the definition of  $\beta$ -mixing coefficients and the geometric ergodicity that

$$
\sup  _ {\boldsymbol {v} \in \mathbb {s} ^ {m l - 1}} | \boldsymbol {v} ^ {\top} \mathbb {e} (\boldsymbol {r} _ {0, t _ {1}} - \boldsymbol {\sigma} _ {t _ {1}}) ^ {\top} (\boldsymbol {r} _ {0, t _ {2}} - \boldsymbol {\sigma} _ {t _ {2}}) \boldsymbol {v} | \preceq \sum_ {j = 1} ^ {m l} \beta (t _ {2} - t _ {1} - 3) \preceq l \rho^ {t _ {2} - t _ {1} - 3},
$$

where the above bound is uniform for any pair  $(t_1, t_2)$  that satisfies  $0 \leq t_1 \leq t_2 - 4$ . therefore, we have

$$
\sum_{\substack{0\leq t_{1} <   t_{2}\leq t - 1\\ |t_{2} - t_{1}|\geq 4}}\sup_{\boldsymbol {v}\in \mathbb{s}^{ml - 1}}|\boldsymbol{v}^{\top}\mathbb{e}(\boldsymbol{r}_{0,t_{1}} - \boldsymbol{\sigma}_{t_{1}})^{\top}(\boldsymbol{r}_{0,t_{2}} - \boldsymbol{\sigma}_{t_{2}})\boldsymbol {v}| \preceq l\sum_{\substack{0\leq t_{1} <   t_{2}\leq t - 1\\ |t_{2} - t_{1}|\geq 4}}\rho^{t_{2} - t_{1} - 3}\preceq lt.
$$

similarly, we can show

$$
\sum_{\substack{0\leq t_{1} <   t_{2}\leq t - 1\\ |t_{2} - t_{1}|\geq 4}}\sup_{\boldsymbol {v}\in \mathbb{s}^{ml - 1}}\left|\boldsymbol{v}^{\top}\mathbb{e}(\boldsymbol{r}_{0,t_{1}} - \boldsymbol{\sigma}_{t_{1}})^{\top}(\boldsymbol{r}_{0,t_{2}} - \boldsymbol{\sigma}_{t_{2}})\boldsymbol {v}\right|\preceq lt,
$$

and hence  $\eta_3\preceq lt$  . this together with (e.74) and (e.75) yields that

$$
\sup  _ {\boldsymbol {v} \in \mathbb {s} ^ {m l - 1}} \mathbb {e} \boldsymbol {v} ^ {\top} \left\{\sum_ {t = 0} ^ {t - 1} \left(\boldsymbol {r} _ {0, t} - \boldsymbol {\sigma}\right) \right\} ^ {\top} \left\{\sum_ {t = 0} ^ {t - 1} \left(\boldsymbol {r} _ {0, t} - \boldsymbol {\sigma}\right) \right\} \boldsymbol {v} \preceq l t. \tag {e.78}
$$

by cauchy-schwarz inequality, we obtain

$$
\sup _ {\boldsymbol {v} \in \mathbb {s} ^ {m l - 1}} \mathbb {e} \left\| \left\{\sum_ {t = 0} ^ {t - 1} (\boldsymbol {r} _ {0, t} - \boldsymbol {\sigma}) \right\} \mathbb {i} (\mathcal {a} _ {0}) \boldsymbol {v} - \mathbb {e} \left\{\sum_ {t = 0} ^ {t - 1} (\boldsymbol {r} _ {0, t} - \boldsymbol {\sigma}) \mathbb {i} (\mathcal {a} _ {0}) \right\} \boldsymbol {v} \right\| _ {2} ^ {2} \preceq l t.
$$

combining this with (e.72), an application of the matrix bernstein inequality (theorem 1.6 in tropp, 2012) yields that

$$
\left\| \sum_ {i = 1} ^ {n} \left\{\sum_ {t = 0} ^ {t - 1} (\pmb {r} _ {i, t} - \pmb {\sigma}) \right\} \mathbb {i} (\mathcal {a} _ {i}) - n \mathbb {e} \left\{\sum_ {t = 0} ^ {t - 1} (\pmb {r} _ {0, t} - \pmb {\sigma}) \right\} \mathbb {i} (\mathcal {a} _ {0}) \right\| _ {2} = o _ {p} \{\sqrt {n t l} \log (n t) \},
$$

under the assumption that  $l = o\{nt / \log^2 (nt)\}$ . this together with (e.72) yields that

$$
\left\| \sum_ {i = 1} ^ {n} \left\{\sum_ {t = 0} ^ {t - 1} \left(\boldsymbol {r} _ {i, t} - \boldsymbol {\sigma}\right) \right\} - n \mathbb {e} \left\{\sum_ {t = 0} ^ {t - 1} \left(\boldsymbol {r} _ {0, t} - \boldsymbol {\sigma}\right) \right\} \mathbb {i} \left(\mathcal {a} _ {0}\right) \right\| _ {2} = o _ {p} \left\{\sqrt {n t l} \log (n t) \right\}. \tag {e.79}
$$

by cauchy-schwarz inequality, we have for any  $\pmb{v}_1, \pmb{v}_2 \in \mathbb{r}^{ml}$  with  $\| \pmb{v}_1 \|_2 = \| \pmb{v}_2 \|_2 = 1$  that

$$
\begin{array}{l} \left| \pmb {v} _ {1} ^ {\top} \mathbb {e} \left\{\sum_ {t = 0} ^ {t - 1} (\pmb {r} _ {0, t} - \pmb {\sigma}) \right\} \mathbb {i} (\mathcal {a} _ {0} ^ {c}) \pmb {v} _ {2} \right| \leq \sqrt {\mathbb {e} \left[ \pmb {v} _ {1} ^ {\top} \left\{\sum_ {t = 0} ^ {t - 1} (\pmb {r} _ {0 , t} - \pmb {\sigma}) \right\} \pmb {v} _ {2} \right] ^ {2}} \sqrt {\operatorname * {p r} (\mathcal {a} _ {0} ^ {c})} \\ \leq \sqrt {\mathbb {e} \left\| \left\{\sum_ {t = 0} ^ {t - 1} (\boldsymbol {r} _ {0 , t} - \boldsymbol {\sigma}) \right\} \boldsymbol {v} _ {2} \right\| _ {2} ^ {2}} \sqrt {\operatorname * {p r} (\mathcal {a} _ {0} ^ {c})} \preceq \sqrt {l t} n ^ {- 3 / 2} t ^ {- 1} = o (n ^ {- 1}), \\ \end{array}
$$

by (e.71), (e.78) and the condition that  $l \ll tn / \log^2(tn)$ . this together with (e.79) yields that

$$
\left\| \sum_ {i = 1} ^ {n} \left\{\sum_ {t = 0} ^ {t - 1} (\pmb {r} _ {i, t} - \mathbb {e} \pmb {r} _ {0, t}) \right\} \right\| _ {2} = o _ {p} \{\sqrt {n t l} \log (n t) \},
$$

and hence  $\| \widehat{\pmb{\sigma}} - \pmb{\sigma} \|_2 = o_p\{l^{1/2}(nt)^{-1/2} \log(nt)\}$ .

part 3: in either scenario, we have shown  $\| \widehat{\pmb{\sigma}} - \pmb{\sigma} \|_2 = o_p\{l^{1/2}(nt)^{-1/2} \log(nt)\}$ . under the condition that  $l = o\{nt / \log(nt)\}$ , we have  $\| \widehat{\pmb{\sigma}} - \pmb{\sigma} \|_2 = o_p(1)$ . by definition, this implies that  $\| \widehat{\pmb{\sigma}} - \pmb{\sigma} \|_2 \leq \bar{c}/6$ , with probability approaching 1. in part 1, we have shown that  $\pmb{v}^\top \pmb{\sigma} \pmb{v} \geq \bar{c} \| \pmb{v} \|_2/2$ , for any  $\pmb{v} \in \mathbb{r}^{ml}$ . by cauchy-schwarz inequality, the following event occurs with probability approaching 1,

$$
\boldsymbol {v} ^ {\top} \widehat {\boldsymbol {\sigma}} \boldsymbol {v} \geq \bar {c} \| \boldsymbol {v} \| _ {2} ^ {2} / 3, \quad \forall \boldsymbol {v} \in \mathbb {r} ^ {m l}.
$$

using similar arguments in part 1, this implies  $\widehat{\sigma}$  is invertible and satisfies  $\| \widehat{\sigma}^{-1}\| _2\leq 3\bar{c}^{-1}$ , with probability tending to 1. therefore

$$
\| \widehat {\pmb {\sigma}} ^ {- 1} - \pmb {\sigma} ^ {- 1} \| _ {2} = \| \widehat {\pmb {\sigma}} ^ {- 1} (\widehat {\pmb {\sigma}} - \pmb {\sigma}) \pmb {\sigma} ^ {- 1} \| _ {2} \leq \| \widehat {\pmb {\sigma}} ^ {- 1} \| _ {2} \| \widehat {\pmb {\sigma}} - \pmb {\sigma} \| _ {2} \| \pmb {\sigma} ^ {- 1} \| _ {2} \leq 6 \bar {c} ^ {- 2} \| \widehat {\pmb {\sigma}} - \pmb {\sigma} \| _ {2},
$$

with probability tending to 1. since  $\| \widehat{\sigma} - \sigma \|_2 = o_p\{l^{1/2}(nt)^{-1/2}\log(nt)\}$ , we obtain  $\| \widehat{\sigma}^{-1} - \sigma^{-1} \|_2 = o_p\{l^{1/2}(nt)^{-1/2}\log(nt)\}$ . the proof is hence completed.

part 4: it suffices to show

$$
\eta_{4}\equiv \max_{t}\sup_{\boldsymbol {v}_{1},\boldsymbol{v}_{2}\in \mathbb{s}^{ml - 1}}\left|\boldsymbol{v}_{1}^{\top}(\mathbb{e}\boldsymbol{r}_{0,t}^{**})\boldsymbol{v}_{2}\right|\preceq 1.
$$

with some calculations, we have

$$
\begin{array}{l} \eta_ {4} \leq \max  _ {t} \sup  _ {\boldsymbol {v} _ {1}, \boldsymbol {v} _ {2} \in \mathbb {s} ^ {m l - 1}} \sum_ {a \in \mathcal {a}} \mathbb {e} | \boldsymbol {v} _ {1} ^ {\top} \boldsymbol {\xi} (x _ {0, t}, a) | | \boldsymbol {v} _ {2} ^ {\top} \boldsymbol {\xi} (x _ {0, t}, a) | b (a | x _ {0, t}) \\ + \max  _ {t} \sup  _ {\boldsymbol {v} _ {1}, \boldsymbol {v} _ {2} \in \mathbb {s} ^ {m l - 1}} \sum_ {a \in \mathcal {a}} \mathbb {e} | \boldsymbol {v} _ {1} ^ {\top} \boldsymbol {\xi} (x _ {0, t}, a) | | \boldsymbol {v} _ {2} ^ {\top} \boldsymbol {u} (x _ {0, t}, a) | b (a | x _ {0, t}) \leq \frac {3}{2} \eta_ {4} ^ {(1)} + \frac {1}{2} \eta_ {4} ^ {(2)}, \\ \end{array}
$$

by cauchy-schwarz inequality, where

$$
\eta_ {4} ^ {(1)} = \max _ {t} \sup _ {\pmb {v} \in \mathbb {s} ^ {m _ {l} - 1}} \sum_ {a \in \mathcal {a}} \mathbb {e} | \pmb {v} ^ {\top} \pmb {\xi} (x _ {0, t}, a) | ^ {2} b (a | x _ {0, t}),
$$

$$
\eta_ {4} ^ {(2)} = \max  _ {t} \sup  _ {\boldsymbol {v} \in \mathbb {s} ^ {m l - 1}} \sum_ {a \in \mathcal {a}} \mathbb {e} | \boldsymbol {v} ^ {\top} \boldsymbol {u} (x _ {0, t}, a) | ^ {2} b (a | x _ {0, t}).
$$

by definition, we have

$$
\eta_ {4} ^ {(1)} = \max _ {t} \lambda_ {\max} \left\{\sum_ {a \in \mathcal {a}} \mathbb {e} \pmb {\xi} (x _ {0, t}, a) \pmb {\xi} ^ {\top} (x _ {0, t}, a) b (a | x _ {0, t}) \right\}.
$$

since the matrix  $\sum_{a\in \mathcal{a}}\pmb {\xi}(x_{0,t},a)\pmb{\xi}^{\top}(x_{0,t},a)b(a|x_{0,t})$  is block diagonal with the main-diagonal blocks  $\{\phi_l(x_{0,t})\phi_l(x_{0,t})^\top b(j|x_{0,t})\}_{j = 1,\dots,m}$ . by lemma 2 and condition a2, we can show  $\eta_4^{(1)}\preceq 1$ . as for  $\eta_4^{(2)}$ , we have

$$
\begin{array}{l} \eta_ {4} ^ {(2)} \leq \max  _ {t} \sup  _ {\boldsymbol {v} \in \mathbb {s} ^ {m l - 1}} \mathbb {e} | \boldsymbol {v} ^ {\top} \boldsymbol {u} (x _ {0, t + 1}) | ^ {2} = \lambda_ {\max } \left\{\mathbb {e} \boldsymbol {u} (x _ {0, t + 1}) \boldsymbol {u} ^ {\top} (x _ {0, t + 1}) \right\} \\ \leq \sup_{\substack{v_{1},\ldots ,v_{m}\in \mathbb{r}^{l}\\ \| v_{1}\|_{2}^{2} + \dots +\| v_{m}\|_{2}^{2} = 1}}\sum_{1\leq j_{1},j_{2}\leq m}v_{j_{1}}^{\top}\mathbb{e}\phi_{l}(x_{0,t + 1})\phi_{l}(x_{0,t + 1})^{\top}v_{j_{2}}\pi (j_{1}|x_{0,t + 1})\pi (j_{2}|x_{0,t + 1}) \\ \leq \lambda_ {\max } \left\{\mathbb {e} \phi_ {l} \left(x _ {0, t + 1}\right) \phi_ {l} \left(x _ {0, t + 1} ^ {*}\right) ^ {\top} \right\}, \\ \end{array}
$$

where the first inequality follows from jensen's inequality. by lemma 2, we can similarly show that  $\eta_4^{(2)}\preceq 1$ . thus, we obtain  $\| \pmb {\sigma}\| _2 = \eta_4\preceq 1$ . the proof is hence completed.

# e.8 proof of lemma 4

the proof of lemma 4 is very similar to that of lemma 3. by condition a3(i), we obtain  $\lambda_{\min}(t^{-1}\sum_{t = 0}^{t - 1}\mathbb{e}\pmb{\xi}_{0,t}\pmb{\xi}_{0,t}^{\top})\geq \bar{c}$ , when  $t$  is bounded. a4(iii) further implies that

$$
\lambda_ {\min } \left[ \int_ {x \in \mathbb {x}} \left\{\sum_ {a \in \mathcal {a}} \boldsymbol {\xi} (x, a) \boldsymbol {\xi} ^ {\top} (x, a) b (a | x) \right\} \right] \geq \bar {c},
$$

as  $t \to \infty$ . using similar arguments as in the first part of the proof of lemma 3, we can show that

$$
\lambda_ {\min } \left(\frac {1}{t} \sum_ {t = 0} ^ {t - 1} \mathbb {e} \boldsymbol {\xi} _ {0, t} \boldsymbol {\xi} _ {0, t} ^ {\top}\right) \geq \frac {\bar {c}}{2}, \tag {e.80}
$$

as either  $n \to \infty$ , or  $t \to \infty$ . using similar arguments in the third part of the proof of lemma 3, we can show that

$$
\left\| \frac {1}{n t} \sum_ {i = 1} ^ {n} \sum_ {t = 0} ^ {t - 1} \boldsymbol {\xi} _ {i, t} \boldsymbol {\xi} _ {i, t} ^ {\top} - \frac {1}{t} \sum_ {t = 0} ^ {t - 1} \mathbb {e} \boldsymbol {\xi} _ {0, t} \boldsymbol {\xi} _ {0, t} ^ {\top} \right\| _ {2} = o _ {p} \{l ^ {1 / 2} (n t) ^ {- 1 / 2} \log (n t) \}. \tag {e.81}
$$

since  $l = o\{\sqrt{nt} / \log(nt)\}$ , it follows from (e.80) and (e.81) that the following event occurs with probability tending to 1,

$$
\begin{array}{l} \lambda_ {\min } \left(\frac {1}{n t} \sum_ {i = 1} ^ {n} \sum_ {t = 0} ^ {t - 1} \boldsymbol {\xi} _ {i, t} \boldsymbol {\xi} _ {i, t} ^ {\top}\right) \geq \lambda_ {\min } \left(\frac {1}{t} \sum_ {t = 0} ^ {t - 1} \mathbb {e} \boldsymbol {\xi} _ {0, t} \boldsymbol {\xi} _ {0, t} ^ {\top}\right) \\ - \left\| \frac {1}{n t} \sum_ {i = 1} ^ {n} \sum_ {t = 0} ^ {t - 1} \boldsymbol {\xi} _ {i, t} \boldsymbol {\xi} _ {i, t} ^ {\top} - \frac {1}{t} \sum_ {t = 0} ^ {t - 1} \mathbb {e} \boldsymbol {\xi} _ {0, t} \boldsymbol {\xi} _ {0, t} ^ {\top} \right\| _ {2} \geq \frac {\bar {c}}{2} - \frac {\bar {c}}{6} = \frac {\bar {c}}{3}. \\ \end{array}
$$

it remains to show  $\lambda_{\mathrm{max}}\{(nt)^{-1}\sum_{i = 1}^{n}\sum_{t = 0}^{t - 1}\pmb{\xi}_{i,t}\pmb{\xi}_{i,t}^{\top}\} = o_p(1)$  and

$$
\lambda_ {\max } \left(\frac {1}{t} \sum_ {t = 0} ^ {t - 1} \mathbb {e} \boldsymbol {\xi} _ {0, t} \boldsymbol {\xi} _ {0, t} ^ {\top}\right) = o (1), \tag {e.82}
$$

suppose (e.82) holds. by (e.81) and the condition that  $l = o\{\sqrt{nt} / \log(nt)\}$ , we have  $\lambda_{\max}\{(nt)^{-1}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\sum_{t=0}^{t-1}\pmb{\xi}_{i,t}\pmb{\xi}_{i,t}^{\top}\} = o_p(1)$ . thus, it suffices to show (e.82). this can be proven using similar arguments in part 2 of the proof of lemma 3. we omit the details for brevity. the proof is hence completed.

# e.9 proof of lemma 5

it follows from cauchy-schwarz inequality and triangle inequality that

$$
\begin{array}{l} \left\| \int_ {x \in \mathbb {x}} \boldsymbol {u} (x) \mathbb {g} (d x) \right\| _ {2} ^ {2} = \sum_ {a = 1} ^ {m} \left\| \int_ {x \in \mathbb {x}} \phi_ {l} (x) \pi (a | x) \mathbb {g} (d x) \right\| _ {2} ^ {2} \\ \geq m ^ {- 1} \left\{\sum_ {a = 1} ^ {m} \left\| \int_ {x \in \mathbb {x}} \phi_ {l} (x) \pi (a | x) \mathbb {g} (d x) \right\| _ {2} \right\} ^ {2} \geq m ^ {- 1} \left\| \int_ {x \in \mathbb {x}} \sum_ {a = 1} ^ {m} \phi_ {l} (x) \pi (a | x) \mathbb {g} (d x) \right\| _ {2} ^ {2} \\ = m ^ {- 1} \left\| \int_ {x \in \mathbb {x}} \phi_ {l} (x) \mathbb {g} (d x) \right\| _ {2} ^ {2}. \\ \end{array}
$$

the proof is hence completed.

# e.10 proof of theorem 2

without loss of generality, we assume  $n = k_{n}n_{\mathrm{min}}$  and  $t = k_{t}t_{\mathrm{min}}$  such that  $|\mathcal{i}_k| = n_{\mathrm{min}}t_{\mathrm{min}}$  for any  $k$ . under the given conditions,  $k$  is bounded. similar to lemma 3, we can show under a4* that

$$
\max  _ {2 \leq k \leq k} \| \boldsymbol {\sigma} _ {\widehat {\pi} _ {\bar {\mathcal {i}} _ {k - 1}}} ^ {- 1} \| _ {2} \leq 2 \bar {c} ^ {- 1}. \tag {e.83}
$$

we next bound the difference between  $\sigma_{\widehat{\pi}_{\bar{\mathcal{i}}_{k - 1}}}$  and  $\widehat{\sigma}_{\mathcal{i}_k,\widehat{\pi}_{\bar{\mathcal{i}}_{k - 1}}}$ . consider the scenario where  $t$  is bounded first. since  $t_{\mathrm{min}} = t$ , the data are divided according to the trajectories they belong to. thus, for any  $k = 2,\ldots ,k$ , variables  $\{(x_{i,t},a_{i,t},y_{i,t},x_{i,t + 1})\}_{(i,t)\in \mathcal{i}_k}$  are independent of  $\{(x_{i,t},a_{i,t},y_{i,t},x_{i,t + 1})\}_{(i,t)\in \bar{\mathcal{i}}_{k - 1}}$ . let

$$
\pmb {\sigma} _ {\widehat {\pi} _ {\bar {\mathcal {i}} _ {k - 1}}} = \mathbb {e} [ \widehat {\pmb {\sigma}} _ {\mathcal {i} _ {k}, \widehat {\pi} _ {\bar {\mathcal {i}} _ {k - 1}}} | \{(x _ {i, t}, a _ {i, t}, y _ {i, t}, x _ {i, t + 1}) \} _ {(i, t) \in \bar {\mathcal {i}} _ {k - 1}} ].
$$

using similar arguments in part 2 of the proof of lemma 3, we can show

$$
\max  _ {2 \leq k \leq k} \| \boldsymbol {\sigma} _ {\widehat {\pi} } - \widehat {\boldsymbol {\sigma}} _ {\mathcal {i} _ {k}, \widehat {\pi} } \| _ {2} \preceq \sqrt {(n t) ^ {- 1} l \log (n t)}, \tag {e.84}
$$

with probability at least  $1 - o(n^{-2}t^{-2}) = 1 - o(1)$ .

now let us consider the scenario where  $t \to \infty$ . for  $k = 1, \ldots, k$ , define  $(i_0(k), t_0(k))$  to be the tuple in  $\mathcal{i}_k$  such that  $i \geq i_0(k), t \geq t_0(k)$  for any  $(i, t) \in \mathcal{i}_k$ . then, we have

$$
\mathcal {i} _ {k} = \{(i, t): i _ {0} (k) \leq i <   i _ {0} (k) + n _ {\min}, t _ {0} (k) \leq t <   t _ {0} (k) + t _ {\min} \}.
$$

consider any  $k \in \{2, \ldots, k\}$  with  $t_0(k) = 0$ ,  $\{(x_{i,t}, a_{i,t}, y_{i,t}, x_{i,t+1})\}_{(i,t) \in \mathcal{i}_k}$  are independent of  $\{(x_{i,t}, a_{i,t}, y_{i,t}, x_{i,t+1})\}_{(i,t) \in \bar{\mathcal{i}}_{k-1}}$ . using similar arguments in part 2 of the proof of lemma 3, we can show wpa1 that,

$$
\max  _ {\substack {k \in \{2, \dots , k \} \\ t _ {0} (k) = 0}} \| \widehat {\boldsymbol {\sigma}} _ {\mathcal {i} _ {k}, \widehat {\pi} _ {\overline {{\mathcal {i}}} _ {k - 1}}} - \boldsymbol {\sigma} _ {\widehat {\pi} _ {\overline {{\mathcal {i}}} _ {k - 1}}} \| _ {2} \preceq \sqrt {\frac {l}{n t}} \log (n t). \tag{e.85}
$$

consider  $k\in \{2,\ldots ,k\}$  with  $t_0(k) > 0$  . we decompose  $\widehat{\sigma}_{\mathcal{t}_k,\widehat{\pi}_{\mathcal{t}}_{k - 1}}$  as

$$
\begin{array}{l} \underbrace {\frac {1}{n _ {\operatorname* {m i n}} t _ {\operatorname* {m i n}}} \sum_ {i = i _ {0} (k)} ^ {i _ {0} (k) + n _ {\operatorname* {m i n}} - 1} \boldsymbol {\xi} _ {i , t _ {0} (k)} \left\{\boldsymbol {\xi} _ {i , t _ {0} (k)} - \gamma \boldsymbol {u} _ {\widehat {\pi} _ {\bar {\mathcal {i}} _ {k - 1}, i , t _ {0} (k) + 1}} \right\} ^ {\top}} _ {\widehat {\sigma} _ {\mathcal {i} _ {k}, \widehat {\pi} _ {\bar {\mathcal {i}} _ {k - 1}}} ^ {(1)}} \\ + \underbrace {\frac {1}{n _ {\mathrm {m i n}} t _ {\mathrm {m i n}}} \sum_ {\stackrel {(i , t) \in \mathcal {i} _ {k}} {t > t _ {0} (k)}} \left[ \pmb {\xi} _ {i , t} \{\pmb {\xi} _ {i , t} - \gamma \pmb {u} _ {\widehat {\pi} _ {\bar {\mathcal {i}} _ {k - 1}}, i, t} \} ^ {\top} \right. .} _ {\widehat {\sigma} _ {\mathcal {i} _ {k}, \widehat {\pi} _ {\bar {\mathcal {i}} _ {k - 1}}} ^ {(2)}} \\ \end{array}
$$

it follows that

$$
\begin{array}{l} \max_{\substack{k\in \{2,\ldots ,k\} \\ t_{0}(k) > 0}}\| \widehat{\boldsymbol{\sigma}}_{\mathcal{i}_{k},\widehat{\boldsymbol{\pi}}_{\bar{\mathcal{i}}_{k - 1}}} - \boldsymbol{\sigma}_{\widehat{\boldsymbol{\pi}}_{\bar{\mathcal{i}}_{k - 1}}}\|_{2}\leq \max_{\substack{k\in \{2,\ldots ,k\} \\ t_{0}(k) > 0}}\| \widehat{\boldsymbol{\sigma}}_{\mathcal{i}_{k},\widehat{\boldsymbol{\pi}}_{\bar{\mathcal{i}}_{k - 1}}}^{(1)} - \boldsymbol{\sigma}_{\widehat{\boldsymbol{\pi}}_{\bar{\mathcal{i}}_{k - 1}}}^{(1)}\|_{2} \\ + \max _ {\substack {k \in \{2, \ldots , k \} \\ t _ {0} (k) > 0}} \| \widehat {\sigma} _ {\mathcal {i} _ {k}, \widehat {\pi} _ {\bar {\mathcal {i}} _ {k - 1}}} ^ {(2)} - \sigma_ {\widehat {\pi} _ {\bar {\mathcal {i}} _ {k - 1}}} ^ {(2)} \| _ {2}. \\ \end{array}
$$

error bound for  $\max_k\| \widehat{\sigma}_{\mathcal{i}_k,\widehat{\pi}_{\bar{\mathcal{i}}_{k - 1}}}^{(1)} - \sigma_{\widehat{\pi}_{\bar{\mathcal{i}}_{k - 1}}}^{(1)}\| _2$  : given  $\{(x_{j,t},a_{j,t},y_{j,t},x_{j,t + 1})\}_{(j,t)\in \bar{\mathcal{i}}_{k - 1}},$ $(a_{i_0(k),t_0(k)},x_{i_0(k),t_0(k) + 1}),\dots ,(a_{i_0(k) + n_{\min} - 1,t_0(k)},x_{i_0(k) + n_{\min} - 1,t_0(k) + 1})$  conditionally independent. using the matrix concentration inequality, we can show wpa1 that

$$
\max  _ {\substack {k \in \{2, \dots , k \} \\ t _ {0} (k) > 0}} \| \widehat {\boldsymbol {\sigma}} _ {\mathcal {i} _ {k}, \widehat {\pi} _ {\bar {\mathcal {i}} _ {k - 1}}} ^ {(1)} - \boldsymbol {\sigma} _ {\widehat {\pi} _ {\bar {\mathcal {i}} _ {k - 1}}} ^ {(1)} \| _ {2} \preceq \sqrt {\frac {l}{n t} \log (n t)}. \tag{e.86}
$$

error bound for  $\max_k\| \widehat{\sigma}_{\mathcal{i}_k,\widehat{\pi}_{\mathcal{i}_{k - 1}}}^{(2)} - \sigma_{\widehat{\pi}_{\mathcal{i}_{k - 1}}}^{(2)}\| _2$  : given  $\{(x_{j,t},a_{j,t},y_{j,t},x_{j,t + 1})\}_{(j,t)\in \bar{\mathcal{i}}_{k - 1}}$  the sets of variables  $\{(x_{i_0(k),t},a_{i_0(k),t},y_{i_0(k),t},x_{i_0(k),t + 1}):t_0(k) + 1\leq t <   t_0(k) + t_{\min}\} ,\dots ,$ $\{(x_{i_0(k) + n_{\min} - 1,t},a_{i_0(k) + n_{\min} - 1,t},y_{i_0(k) + n_{\min} - 1,t},x_{i_0(k) + n_{\min} - 1,t + 1}):t_0(k) + 1\leq t <   t_0(k) + t_{\min}\}$  are conditionally independent. moreover, for any  $i$  such that  $(i,t_0(k))\in \mathcal{i}_k$  , the density function of  $x_{i,t_0(k) + 1}$  conditional on  $\{(x_{j,t},a_{j,t},y_{j,t},x_{j,t + 1})\}_{(j,t)\in \bar{\mathcal{i}}_{k - 1}}$  is uniformly bounded under a3. using similar arguments in bounding  $\| \widehat{\sigma} -\sigma \| _2$  in part 2 of the proof of lemma 3, we can show wpa1 that

$$
\max  _ {\substack {k \in \{2, \dots , k \} \\ t _ {0} (k) > 0}} \| \widehat {\boldsymbol {\sigma}} _ {\mathcal {i} _ {k}, \widehat {\pi} _ {\bar {\mathcal {i}} _ {k - 1}}} ^ {(2)} - \boldsymbol {\sigma} _ {\widehat {\pi} _ {\bar {\mathcal {i}} _ {k - 1}}} ^ {(2)} \| _ {2} \preceq \sqrt {\frac {l}{n t}} \log (n t). \tag{e.87}
$$

combining (e.86) with (e.87) and (e.85), we obtain wpa1 that

$$
\max  _ {k \in \{2, \dots , k \}} \| \widehat {\boldsymbol {\sigma}} _ {\mathcal {i} _ {k}, \widehat {\pi} _ {\bar {\mathcal {i}} _ {k - 1}}} - \boldsymbol {\sigma} _ {\widehat {\pi} _ {\bar {\mathcal {i}} _ {k - 1}}} \| _ {2} \preceq \sqrt {\frac {l}{n t}} \log (n t). \tag {e.88}
$$

to summarize, we have shown that

$$
\max _ {k \in \{2, \dots , k \}} \| \boldsymbol {\sigma} _ {\widehat {\pi} _ {\bar {\mathcal {i}} _ {k - 1}}} ^ {- 1} \| _ {2} \leq 2 \bar {c} ^ {- 1} \mathrm {a n d} \max _ {k \in \{2, \dots , k \}} \| \widehat {\boldsymbol {\sigma}} _ {\mathcal {i} _ {k}, \widehat {\pi} _ {\bar {\mathcal {i}} _ {k - 1}}} - \boldsymbol {\sigma} _ {\widehat {\pi} _ {\bar {\mathcal {i}} _ {k - 1}}} \| _ {2} \preceq \sqrt {\frac {l}{n t}} \log (n t),
$$

wpa1, regardless of whether  $t$  is bounded or not. under the given conditions, we have  $\sqrt{l / (nt)}\log (nt) = o(1)$ . using similar arguments in the proof of lemma 3, we can show wpa1 that

$$
\max _ {k \in \{2, \dots , k \}} \| \widehat {\pmb {\sigma}} _ {\mathcal {i} _ {k}, \widehat {\pi} _ {\bar {\mathcal {i}} _ {k - 1}}} ^ {- 1} \| _ {2} \leq \frac {3}{\bar {c}}, \max _ {k \in \{2, \dots , k \}} \| \widehat {\pmb {\sigma}} _ {\mathcal {i} _ {k}, \widehat {\pi} _ {\bar {\mathcal {i}} _ {k - 1}}} ^ {- 1} - \pmb {\sigma} _ {\widehat {\pi} _ {\bar {\mathcal {i}} _ {k - 1}}} ^ {- 1} \| _ {2} \preceq \sqrt {\frac {l}{n t}} \log (n t). \quad (\mathrm {e}. 8 9)
$$

notice that  $\widehat{\pi} = \widehat{\pi}_{\overline{\mathcal{i}}(k)}$ . by lemma 1, we have  $q(\widehat{\pi}_{\overline{\mathcal{i}}(k)};\cdot,a) \in \lambda(p,c')$  for any  $k \in \{1,\ldots,k\}$ . using similar arguments in the proof of theorem 12.8 of schumaker (1981) and proof of proposition 5 of meyer (1992), there exist some vectors  $\{\beta_{\widehat{\pi}_{\overline{\mathcal{i}}(k)},a}^{*}\}_{a \in \mathcal{a},1 \leq k \leq k}$  that satisfy

$$
\max  _ {1 \leq k \leq k} \sup  _ {x \in \mathbb {x}, a \in \mathcal {a}} | q (\widehat {\pi_ {\bar {\mathcal {i}} (k)}}; x, a) - \phi_ {l} ^ {\top} (x) \beta_ {\widehat {\pi_ {\bar {\mathcal {i}} (k)}}}, a | \leq c l ^ {- p / d}, \tag {e.90}
$$

for some constant  $c > 0$ . similar to (e.43), we have by (e.90) that

$$
\max  _ {(i, t) \in \mathcal {i} _ {k}} | r _ {i, t} | \leq 2 c l ^ {- p / d}, \forall k = 2, \dots , k, \tag {e.91}
$$

where

$$
\begin{array}{l} r _ {i, t} = \gamma \sum_ {a \in \mathcal {a}} \{\phi_ {l} ^ {\top} (x _ {i, t + 1}) \beta_ {\widehat {\pi} _ {\bar {\mathcal {i}} _ {k - 1}}, a} ^ {*} - q (\widehat {\pi} _ {\bar {\mathcal {i}} _ {k - 1}}; x _ {i, t + 1}, a) \} \widehat {\pi} _ {\bar {\mathcal {i}} _ {k - 1}} (a | x _ {i, t + 1}) \\ - \{\phi_ {l} ^ {\top} (x _ {i, t}) \beta_ {\widehat {\pi} _ {\bar {\mathcal {i}} _ {k - 1}}, a _ {i, t}} ^ {*} - q (\widehat {\pi} _ {\bar {\mathcal {i}} _ {k - 1}}; x _ {i, t}, a _ {i, t}) \}. \\ \end{array}
$$

similar to the proof of theorem 1, we have by (e.89) and (e.91) that

$$
\widehat {\boldsymbol {\beta}} _ {\mathcal {i} _ {k}, \widehat {\pi} _ {\bar {\mathcal {i}} _ {k - 1}}} - \boldsymbol {\beta} _ {\widehat {\pi} _ {\bar {\mathcal {i}} _ {k - 1}}} ^ {*} = \underbrace {\boldsymbol {\sigma} _ {\widehat {\pi} _ {\bar {\mathcal {i}} _ {k - 1}}} ^ {- 1} \left(\frac {k}{n t} \sum_ {(i , t) \in \mathcal {i} _ {k}} \boldsymbol {\xi} _ {i , t} \varepsilon_ {i , t}\right)} _ {\zeta_ {1, k}} + o (l ^ {- p / d}) + o _ {p} \{l (n t) ^ {- 1} \log (n t) \},
$$

where

$$
\varepsilon_ {i, t} = y _ {i, t} + \gamma \sum_ {a \in \mathcal {a}} q (\widehat {\pi} _ {\bar {\mathcal {i}} _ {k - 1}}; x _ {i, t + 1}, a) \widehat {\pi} _ {\bar {\mathcal {i}} _ {k - 1}} (a | x _ {i, t + 1}) - q (\widehat {\pi} _ {\bar {\mathcal {i}} _ {k - 1}}; x _ {i, t}, a _ {i, t}),
$$

for any  $(i,t)\in \mathcal{i}_k$

to prove the asymptotic normality of  $\sqrt{nt(k - 1) / k}\widetilde{\sigma}^{-1}(\mathbb{g})\{\widetilde{v} (\mathbb{g}) - v(\widehat{\pi};\mathbb{g})\}$ , it suffices to show

$$
\sqrt {\frac {n t k}{k - 1}} \sum_ {k = 2} ^ {k} \frac {\widehat {v} _ {\mathcal {i} _ {k}} \left(\widehat {\pi} _ {\bar {\mathcal {i}} _ {k - 1}} ; \mathbb {g}\right) - v \left(\widehat {\pi} ; \mathbb {g}\right)}{\widehat {\sigma} _ {\mathcal {i} _ {k}} \left(\widehat {\pi} _ {\bar {\mathcal {i}} _ {k - 1}} ; \mathbb {g}\right)} \xrightarrow {d} n (0, 1). \tag {e.92}
$$

using similar arguments in the proof of theorem 1, we can show

$$
\begin{array}{l} \left| \sqrt {\frac {n t k}{k - 1}} \sum_ {k = 2} ^ {k} \frac {\widehat {v} _ {\mathcal {i} _ {k}} \left(\widehat {\pi} _ {\bar {\mathcal {i}} _ {k - 1}} ; \mathbb {g}\right) - v \left(\widehat {\pi} ; \mathbb {g}\right)}{\widehat {\sigma} _ {\mathcal {i} _ {k}} \left(\widehat {\pi} _ {\bar {\mathcal {i}} _ {k - 1}} ; \mathbb {g}\right)} \right. \tag {e.93} \\ - \sqrt {\frac {n t k}{k - 1}} \sum_ {k = 2} ^ {k} \frac {v (\widehat {\pi} _ {\bar {\mathcal {i}} _ {k - 1}} ; \mathbb {g}) + \left\{\int_ {x} u _ {\widehat {\pi} _ {\bar {\mathcal {i}} _ {k - 1}}} (x) \mathbb {g} (d x) \right\} ^ {\top} \zeta_ {1 , k} - v (\widehat {\pi} ; \mathbb {g})}{\widehat {\sigma} _ {\mathcal {i} _ {k}} (\widehat {\pi} _ {\bar {\mathcal {i}} _ {k - 1}} ; \mathbb {g})} \bigg | = o _ {p} (1). \\ \end{array}
$$

notice that  $\widehat{\pi} = \widehat{\pi}_{\overline{\mathcal{i}}_k}$ . under a5, we have

$$
\begin{array}{l} \frac {1}{k - 1} \sum_ {k = 2} ^ {k} \mathbb {e} | v (\widehat {\pi} _ {\bar {\mathcal {i}} _ {k - 1}}; \mathbb {g}) - v (\widehat {\pi}; \mathbb {g}) | \\ \leq \frac {1}{k - 1} \sum_ {k = 2} ^ {k} \mathbb {e} | v (\widehat {\pi} _ {\bar {\mathcal {i}} _ {k - 1}}; \mathbb {g}) - v (\pi^ {*}; \mathbb {g}) | + \mathbb {e} | v (\widehat {\pi}; \mathbb {g}) - v (\pi^ {*}; \mathbb {g}) | \\ \leq o (1) \left\{\frac {1}{k - 1} \sum_ {k = 1} ^ {k - 1} (n t) ^ {- b _ {0}} k ^ {- b _ {0}} k ^ {b _ {0}} + (n t) ^ {- b _ {0}} \right\}, \\ \end{array}
$$

where  $o(1)$  denotes some positive constant. since  $\sum_{k=1}^{k-1} k^{-b_0} \leq 1 + \int_1^k x^{-b_0} dx \preceq k^{1-b_0}$ , we obtain that

$$
\frac {1}{k - 1} \sum_ {k = 2} ^ {k} \mathbb {e} | v (\widehat {\pi} _ {\bar {\mathcal {i}} _ {k - 1}}; \mathbb {g}) - v (\widehat {\pi}; \mathbb {g}) | = o \{(n t) ^ {- b _ {0}} \},
$$

and hence

$$
\sqrt {\frac {n t k}{k - 1}} \sum_ {k = 2} ^ {k} \mathbb {e} | v (\widehat {\pi} _ {\mathcal {i} _ {k - 1}}; \mathbb {g}) - v (\widehat {\pi}; \mathbb {g}) | = o \{(n t) ^ {1 / 2 - b _ {0}} \} = o \left\{\left\| \int_ {x} \phi_ {l} (x) d x \right\| _ {2} \right\},
$$

by a6. by markov's inequality, we obtain that

$$
\left\| \int_ {x} \phi_ {l} (x) d x \right\| _ {2} ^ {- 1} \frac {\sqrt {n t k}}{k - 1} \sum_ {k = 2} ^ {k} | v (\widehat {\pi} _ {\bar {\mathcal {i}} _ {k - 1}}; \mathbb {g}) - v (\widehat {\pi}; \mathbb {g}) | = o _ {p} (1).
$$

similar to lemma 5, we can show that for any  $k \in \{2, \ldots, k\}$ ,

$$
\left\| \int_ {x} \phi_ {l} (x) d x \right\| _ {2} ^ {- 1} \geq m ^ {- 1 / 2} \left\| \int_ {x} \boldsymbol {u} _ {\hat {\pi} _ {\bar {\mathcal {i}} _ {k - 1}}} (x) d x \right\| _ {2} ^ {- 1}.
$$

it follows that

$$
\sqrt {\frac {n t k}{k - 1}} \sum_ {k = 2} ^ {k} \left\| \int_ {x} \boldsymbol {u} _ {\widehat {\pi} _ {\bar {\mathcal {i}} _ {k - 1}}} (x) d x \right\| _ {2} ^ {- 1} | v (\widehat {\pi} _ {\bar {\mathcal {i}} _ {k - 1}}; \mathbb {g}) - v (\widehat {\pi}; \mathbb {g}) | = o _ {p} (1). \tag {e.94}
$$

using similar arguments in the proof of theorem 1, we can show

$$
\max _ {k \in \{2, \dots , k \}} \left| \frac {\widehat {\sigma} _ {\mathcal {i} _ {k}} ^ {2} (\widehat {\pi} _ {\bar {\mathcal {i}} _ {k - 1}} ; \mathbb {g})}{\sigma^ {2} (\widehat {\pi} _ {\bar {\mathcal {i}} _ {k - 1}} ; \mathbb {g})} - 1 \right| = o _ {p} (1).
$$

since

$$
\left| \frac {\widehat {\sigma} _ {\mathcal {i} _ {k}} (\widehat {\pi} _ {\bar {\mathcal {i}} _ {k - 1}} ; \mathbb {g})}{\sigma (\widehat {\pi} _ {\bar {\mathcal {i}} _ {k - 1}} ; \mathbb {g})} - 1 \right| = \left| \frac {\widehat {\sigma} _ {\mathcal {i} _ {k}} ^ {2} (\widehat {\pi} _ {\bar {\mathcal {i}} _ {k - 1}} ; \mathbb {g})}{\sigma^ {2} (\widehat {\pi} _ {\bar {\mathcal {i}} _ {k - 1}} ; \mathbb {g})} - 1 \right| \left| \frac {\widehat {\sigma} _ {\mathcal {i} _ {k}} (\widehat {\pi} _ {\bar {\mathcal {i}} _ {k - 1}} ; \mathbb {g})}{\sigma (\widehat {\pi} _ {\bar {\mathcal {i}} _ {k - 1}} ; \mathbb {g})} + 1 \right| ^ {- 1},
$$

we obtain

$$
\max  _ {k \in \{2, \dots , k \}} \left| \frac {\widehat {\sigma} _ {\mathcal {i} _ {k}} \left(\widehat {\pi} _ {\overline {{\mathcal {i}}} _ {k - 1}} ; \mathbb {g}\right)}{\sigma \left(\widehat {\pi} _ {\overline {{\mathcal {i}}} _ {k - 1}} ; \mathbb {g}\right)} - 1 \right| = o _ {p} (1). \tag {e.95}
$$

similar to (e.50), we can show there exists some constant  $c_6 > 0$ , such that the following occurs wpa1,

$$
\sigma (\widehat {\pi} _ {\bar {\mathcal {i}} _ {k - 1}}; \mathbb {g}) \geq c _ {6} \left\| \int_ {x} \boldsymbol {u} _ {\widehat {\pi} _ {\bar {\mathcal {i}} _ {k - 1}}} (x) \mathbb {g} (d x) \right\| _ {2}, \forall k \in \{2, \ldots , k \}.
$$

this together with (e.95) yields that

$$
\widehat {\sigma} _ {\mathcal {i} _ {k}} (\widehat {\pi} _ {\bar {\mathcal {i}} _ {k - 1}}; \mathbb {g}) \geq c _ {6} \left\| \int_ {x} \boldsymbol {u} _ {\widehat {\pi} _ {\bar {\mathcal {i}} _ {k - 1}}} (x) \mathbb {g} (d x) \right\| _ {2}, \forall k \in \{2, \ldots , k \},
$$

wpal. in view of (e.94), we obtain

$$
\left| \sqrt {\frac {n t k}{k - 1}} \sum_ {k = 2} ^ {k} \frac {v (\widehat {\pi} _ {\bar {\mathcal {i}} _ {k - 1}} ; \mathbb {g}) - v (\widehat {\pi} ; \mathbb {g})}{\widehat {\sigma} _ {\mathcal {i} _ {k}} (\widehat {\pi} _ {\bar {\mathcal {i}} _ {k - 1}} ; \mathbb {g})} \right| = o _ {p} (1).
$$

combining this together with (e.93), we obtain that

$$
\sqrt {\frac {n t k}{k - 1}} \sum_ {k = 2} ^ {k} \frac {\widehat {v} _ {\mathcal {i} _ {k}} (\widehat {\pi} _ {\bar {\mathcal {i}} _ {k - 1}} ; \mathbb {g}) - v (\widehat {\pi} ; \mathbb {g})}{\widehat {\sigma} _ {\mathcal {i} _ {k}} (\widehat {\pi} _ {\bar {\mathcal {i}} _ {k - 1}} ; \mathbb {g})} - \sqrt {\frac {n t k}{k - 1}} \sum_ {k = 2} ^ {k} \frac {\{\int_ {x} u _ {\widehat {\pi} _ {\bar {\mathcal {i}} _ {k - 1}}} (x) \mathbb {g} (d x) \} ^ {\top} \zeta_ {1 , k}}{\widehat {\sigma} _ {\mathcal {i} _ {k}} (\widehat {\pi} _ {\bar {\mathcal {i}} _ {k - 1}} ; \mathbb {g})} = o _ {p} (1).
$$

to prove (e.92), it suffices to show

$$
\sqrt {\frac {n t k}{k - 1}} \sum_ {k = 2} ^ {k} \frac {\left\{\int_ {x} \boldsymbol {u} _ {\widehat {\pi} _ {\bar {\mathcal {i}} _ {k - 1}}} (x) \mathbb {g} (d x) \right\} ^ {\top} \zeta_ {1 , k}}{\widehat {\sigma} _ {\mathcal {i} _ {k}} \left(\widehat {\pi} _ {\bar {\mathcal {i}} _ {k - 1}} ; \mathbb {g}\right)} \overset {d} {\rightarrow} n (0, 1). \tag {e.96}
$$

the lhs of (e.96) can be further decomposed as

$$
\begin{array}{l} \underbrace {\sqrt {\frac {n t k}{k - 1}} \sum_ {k = 2} ^ {k} \frac {\{\int_ {x} \pmb {u} _ {\widehat {\pi} _ {\bar {\mathcal {i}} _ {k - 1}}} (x) \mathbb {g} (d x) \} ^ {\top} \zeta_ {1 , k}}{\sigma (\widehat {\pi} _ {\bar {\mathcal {i}} _ {k - 1}} ; \mathbb {g})}} _ {\eta_ {7}} \\ + \underbrace {\sqrt {\frac {n t k}{k - 1}} \sum_ {k = 2} ^ {k} \frac {\left\{\int_ {x} \boldsymbol {u} _ {\widehat {\pi} _ {\bar {\mathcal {i}} _ {k - 1}}} (x) \mathbb {g} (d x) \right\} ^ {\top} \zeta_ {1 , k}}{\sigma (\widehat {\pi} _ {\bar {\mathcal {i}} _ {k - 1}} ; \mathbb {g})} \left\{\frac {\sigma (\widehat {\pi} _ {\bar {\mathcal {i}} _ {k - 1}} ; \mathbb {g})}{\widehat {\sigma} _ {\mathcal {i} _ {k}} (\widehat {\pi} _ {\bar {\mathcal {i}} _ {k - 1}} ; \mathbb {g})} - 1 \right\}} _ {\eta_ {8}} . \\ \end{array}
$$

in the following, we show  $\eta_7 \xrightarrow{d} n(0,1)$ . based on (e.94), one can show  $\eta_8 \xrightarrow{p} 0$ . assertion (e.96) thus follows from slutsky's theorem.

notice that  $\eta_7$  equals

$$
\eta_ {7} = \sqrt {\frac {k}{n t (k - 1)}} \sum_ {k = 2} ^ {k} \sum_ {(i, t) \in \mathcal {i} _ {k}} \frac {\{\int_ {x} \boldsymbol {u} _ {\widehat {\pi} _ {\bar {\mathcal {i}} _ {k - 1}}} (x) \mathbb {g} (d x) \} ^ {\top} \boldsymbol {\sigma} _ {\widehat {\pi} _ {\bar {\mathcal {i}} _ {k - 1}}} ^ {- 1} \boldsymbol {\xi} _ {i , t} \varepsilon_ {i , t}}{\sigma (\widehat {\pi} _ {\bar {\mathcal {i}} _ {k - 1}} ; \mathbb {g})}.
$$

for any  $1 \leq g \leq nt$ , there exists some integer  $k(g)$  that satisfies  $\{k(g) - 1\} n_{\min} t_{\min} + 1 \leq g \leq k(g) n_{\min} t_{\min}$ . let  $t(g)$  and  $i(g)$  be the integers that satisfy

$$
g - \{k (g) - 1 \} n _ {\min} t _ {\min} = \{i (g) - i _ {0} (k (g)) \} t _ {\min} + t (g) - t _ {0} (k (g)) + 1.
$$

let  $\mathcal{f}^{(0)} = \{x_{1,0},a_{1,0}\}$ . then we iteratively define  $\{\mathcal{f}^{(g)}\}_{1\leq g\leq nt}$  as follows:

$$
\mathcal {f} ^ {(g)} = \mathcal {f} ^ {(g - 1)} \cup \{y _ {i (g), t - 1}, x _ {i (g), t} \}, \mathrm {i f} t (g + 1) > 0 \& t (g) = t - 1,
$$

$$
\mathcal {f} ^ {(g)} = \mathcal {f} ^ {(g - 1)} \cup \{y _ {i (g), t (g)}, x _ {i (g), t (g) + 1}, a _ {i (g), t (g) + 1} \}, \mathrm {i f} t (g + 1) > 0 \& t (g) <   t - 1,
$$

$$
\mathcal {f} ^ {(g)} = \mathcal {f} ^ {(g - 1)} \cup \{y _ {i (g), t - 1}, x _ {i (g), t}, x _ {i (g + 1), 0}, a _ {i (g + 1), 0} \}, \mathrm {i f} t (g + 1) = 0 \& t (g) = t - 1,
$$

$$
\mathcal {f} ^ {(g)} = \mathcal {f} ^ {(g - 1)} \cup \left\{y _ {i (g), t (g)}, x _ {i (g), t (g) + 1}, a _ {i (g), t (g) + 1}, x _ {i (g + 1), 0}, a _ {i (g + 1), 0} \right\}, \quad \text {o t h e r w i s e}.
$$

let  $\pmb{\xi}^{(g)} = \pmb{\xi}_{i(g),t(g)}$  and  $\varepsilon^{(g)} = \varepsilon_{i(g),t(g)}$ . we rewrite  $\eta_7$  as

$$
\eta_ {7} = \sqrt {\frac {k}{n t (k - 1)}} \sum_ {g = n t / k + 1} ^ {n t} \frac {\{\int_ {x} \pmb {u} _ {\widehat {\pi} _ {\bar {\mathcal {i}} (k (g) - 1)}} (x) \mathbb {g} (d x) \} ^ {\top} \pmb {\sigma} _ {\widehat {\pi} _ {\bar {\mathcal {i}} (k (g) - 1)}} ^ {- 1} \pmb {\xi} ^ {(g)} _ {\mathcal {e}} ^ {(g)}}{\sigma (\widehat {\pi} _ {\bar {\mathcal {i}} (k (g) - 1)} ; \mathbb {g})}.
$$

one can show that  $\eta_7$  forms a mean-zero martingale with respect to the filtration  $\{\sigma(\mathcal{f}^{(g)})\}_{g \geq nt / k}$ . using similar arguments in the proof of theorem 1, we can show that

$$
\max  _ {g \in \{n t / k + 1, \dots , n t \}} \left| \frac {\left\{\int_ {x} \boldsymbol {u} _ {\widehat {\pi} _ {\bar {\mathcal {i}} (k (g) - 1)}} (x) \mathbb {g} (d x) \right\} ^ {\top} \boldsymbol {\sigma} _ {\widehat {\pi} _ {\bar {\mathcal {i}} (k (g) - 1)}} ^ {- 1} \boldsymbol {\xi} ^ {(g)} \varepsilon^ {(g)}}{\sigma \left(\widehat {\pi} _ {\bar {\mathcal {i}} (k (g) - 1)} ; \mathbb {g}\right)} \right| \stackrel {{p}} {{\rightarrow}} 0 \tag {e.97}
$$

moreover, we have

$$
\begin{array}{l} \left| \frac {k}{n t (k - 1)} \sum_ {g = n t / k + 1} ^ {n t} \frac {[ \{\int_ {x} \boldsymbol {u} _ {\widehat {\pi} _ {\bar {\mathcal {i}} (k (g) - 1)}} (x) \mathbb {g} (d x) \} ^ {\top} \boldsymbol {\sigma} _ {\widehat {\pi} _ {\bar {\mathcal {i}} (k (g) - 1)}} ^ {- 1} \boldsymbol {\xi} ^ {(g)} _ {\mathcal {e}} ^ {(g)} ] ^ {2}}{\sigma^ {2} (\widehat {\pi} _ {\bar {\mathcal {i}} (k (g) - 1)} ; \mathbb {g})} - 1 \right| \\ \leq \max _ {k \in \{2, \dots , k \}} \left| \frac {k}{n t} \sum_ {(i, t) \in \mathcal {i} _ {k}} \frac {[ \{\int_ {x} \pmb {u} _ {\widehat {\pi} _ {\bar {\mathcal {i}} _ {k - 1}}} (x) \mathbb {g} (d x) \} ^ {\top} \pmb {\sigma} _ {\widehat {\pi} _ {\bar {\mathcal {i}} _ {k - 1}}} ^ {- 1} \pmb {\xi} _ {i , t} \varepsilon_ {i , t} ] ^ {2}}{\sigma^ {2} (\widehat {\pi} _ {\bar {\mathcal {i}} _ {k - 1}} ; \mathbb {g})} - 1 \right| \\ \leq \max _ {k \in \{2, \ldots , k \}} \frac {\| \{\int_ {x} \boldsymbol {u} _ {\widehat {\pi} _ {\bar {\mathcal {i}} _ {k - 1}}} (x) \mathbb {g} (d x) \} ^ {\top} \boldsymbol {\sigma} _ {\widehat {\pi} _ {\bar {\mathcal {i}} _ {k - 1}}} ^ {- 1} \| _ {2} ^ {2} \| \widehat {\omega} _ {\mathcal {i} _ {k} , \widehat {\pi} _ {\bar {\mathcal {i}} _ {k - 1}}} ^ {*} - \boldsymbol {\omega} _ {\widehat {\pi} _ {\bar {\mathcal {i}} _ {k - 1}}} \| _ {2}}{\sigma^ {2} (\widehat {\pi} _ {\bar {\mathcal {i}} _ {k - 1}} ; \mathbb {g})}, \\ \end{array}
$$

where  $\widehat{\pmb{\omega}}_{\mathcal{i}_k,\widehat{\pi}_{\widehat{\mathcal{i}}_{k - 1}}}^* = |\mathcal{i}_k|^{-1}\sum_{(i,t)\in \mathcal{i}_k}\pmb {\xi}_{i,t}\pmb{\xi}_{i,t}^\top \varepsilon_{i,t}^2.$

similar to the proof of theorem 1, we can show  $\max_{k\in \{2,\dots,k\}}\| \widehat{\omega}_{\mathcal{i}_k,\widehat{\pi}_{\bar{\mathcal{i}}_{k - 1}}}^*$ $-\omega_{\widehat{\pi}_{\bar{\mathcal{i}}_{k - 1}}}\| _2 = o_p(1)$ . similar to (e.49), we can show there exists some constant  $c_{7} > 0$  such that

$$
\max  _ {k \in \{2, \dots , k \}} \frac {\| \left\{\int_ {x} \boldsymbol {u} _ {\widehat {\pi} _ {\bar {\mathcal {i}} _ {k - 1}}} (x) \mathbb {g} (d x) \right\} ^ {\top} \boldsymbol {\sigma} _ {\widehat {\pi} _ {\bar {\mathcal {i}} _ {k - 1}}} ^ {- 1} \| _ {2} ^ {2}}{\sigma^ {2} \left(\widehat {\pi} _ {\bar {\mathcal {i}} _ {k - 1}} ; \mathbb {g}\right)} \leq c _ {7}. \tag {e.98}
$$

it follows that

$$
\left| \frac {k}{n t (k - 1)} \sum_ {g = n t / k + 1} ^ {n t} \frac {\left[\left\{\int_ {x} \boldsymbol {u} _ {\widehat {\pi} _ {\bar {\mathcal {i}} (k (g) - 1)}} (x) \mathbb {g} (d x) \right\} ^ {\top} \boldsymbol {\sigma} _ {\widehat {\pi} _ {\bar {\mathcal {i}} (k (g) - 1)}} ^ {- 1} \boldsymbol {\xi} ^ {(g)} \varepsilon^ {(g)} \right] ^ {2}}{\sigma^ {2} (\widehat {\pi} _ {\bar {\mathcal {i}} (k (g) - 1)} ; \mathbb {g})} - 1 \right| \stackrel {{p}} {{\rightarrow}} 0. \quad (\mathrm {e}. 9 9)
$$

using a martingale central limit theorem for triangular arrays (corollary 2.8 of mcleish, 1974), we have by (e.97) and (e.99) that  $\eta_7 \xrightarrow{d} n(0,1)$ . the proof is hence completed.

# e.11 proof of theorem 3

based on the discussions in section 3.2.3, it suffices to show

$$
\mathbb {e} \int_ {x} \sum_ {a \in \mathcal {a}} q ^ {o p t} (x, a) \{\pi^ {o p t} (a | x) - \widehat {\pi} _ {\mathcal {i}} (a | x) \} \mathbb {g} (d x) = o (| \mathcal {i} | ^ {- b _ {*} (1 + \alpha)}),
$$

and

$$
\mathbb {e} \int_ {x} \sum_ {a \in \mathcal {a}} q ^ {o p t} (x, a) \{\pi^ {o p t} (a | x) - \widehat {\pi} _ {\mathcal {i}} (a | x) \} d x = o (| \mathcal {i} | ^ {- b _ {*} (1 + \alpha)}). \qquad \mathrm {(e . 1 0 0)}
$$

we only prove (e.100) for brevity. under the given conditions, we have  $\operatorname{pr}(\mathcal{a}_0) \geq 1 - o(|\mathcal{i}|^{-\kappa})$ , where

$$
\mathcal {a} _ {0} = \left\{\sup  _ {x \in \mathbb {x}, a \in \mathcal {a}} | \widehat {q} _ {\mathcal {i}} (x, a) - q ^ {o p t} (x, a) | \leq c | \mathcal {i} | ^ {- b _ {*}} \right\}.
$$

notice that

$$
\begin{array}{l} \mathbb {e} \int_ {x} \sum_ {a \in \mathcal {a}} q ^ {o p t} (x, a) \{\pi^ {o p t} (a | x) - \widehat {\pi} _ {\mathcal {i}} (a | x) \} d x \\ = \mathbb {e} \int_ {x} \sum_ {a \in \mathcal {a}} q ^ {\text {o p t}} (x, a) \left\{\pi^ {\text {o p t}} (a | x) - \widehat {\pi} _ {\mathcal {i}} (a | x) \right\} d x \mathbb {i} \left(\mathcal {a} _ {0} ^ {c}\right) \tag {e.101} \\ { + } { \mathbb { e } \int _ { x } \sum _ { a \in \mathcal { a } } q ^ { o p t } ( x , a ) \{ \pi ^ { o p t } ( a | x ) - \widehat { \pi } _ { \mathcal { i } } ( a | x ) \} d x \mathbb { i } ( \mathcal { a } _ { 0 } ) . } \\ \end{array}
$$

under a1,  $q^{opt}$  is uniformly bounded. therefore, the first term on the rhs of (e.101) is upper bounded by  $o\{\operatorname{pr}(\mathcal{a}_0^c)\} = o(|\mathcal{i}|^{-\kappa})$ . since  $\kappa$  can be chosen arbitrarily large, it suffices to show

$$
\mathbb {e} \int_ {x} \sum_ {a \in \mathcal {a}} q ^ {o p t} (x, a) \left\{\pi^ {o p t} (a | x) - \widehat {\pi} _ {\mathcal {i}} (a | x) \right\} d x \mathbb {i} (\mathcal {a} _ {0}) = o \{| \mathcal {i} | ^ {- b _ {*} (1 + \alpha)} \}. \tag {e.102}
$$

for any  $x\in \mathbb{x}$  , suppose

$$
\max  _ {a} q ^ {\text {o p t}} (x, a) - \max  _ {a \in \mathcal {a} - \arg \max  _ {a ^ {\prime}} q ^ {\text {o p t}} \left(x, a ^ {\prime}\right)} q ^ {\text {o p t}} (x, a) > 2 c | \mathcal {i} | ^ {- b _ {*}}. \tag {e.103}
$$

under the event defined in  $\mathcal{a}_0$ , we have

$$
\max _ {a} \widehat {q} _ {\mathcal {i}} (x, a) - \max _ {a \in \mathcal {a} - \arg \max _ {a ^ {\prime}} q ^ {o p t} (x, a ^ {\prime})} \widehat {q} _ {\mathcal {i}} (x, a) > 0,
$$

and hence

$$
\{a \in \mathcal {a}: \widehat {\pi} (a | x) = 1 \} \subseteq \underset {a \in \mathcal {a}} {\arg \max } q ^ {o p t} (x, a).
$$

thus, we have

$$
\sum_ {a \in \mathcal {a}} q ^ {o p t} (x, a) \{\pi^ {o p t} (a | x) - \widehat {\pi} _ {\mathcal {i}} (a | x) \} = 0,
$$

when (e.103) holds. let  $\mathbb{x}_*$  denote the set of  $x$  that satisfies (e.103). it follows that

$$
